Recurring features: "Let's say it!" phonics pages, "Let's write!"/"Let's check!"/"LOOK" grammar boxes, a "Look, say, cover, write, check" spelling grid in every unit, guided writing from models, and end-of-book spelling-test grids and cut-out game cards.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 (pp. 4–19).** Written practice for the Pupil's Book Unit 1: read-and-write-about-yourself from Nadia's model; sentence matching; a phonics page on initial blends (bl, fl, pl, sl — *black, plane, sleep, flag, flower, plant*); write-about-yourself from Lina's text; *Do you like the slide? — Yes, I do / No, I don't* survey; matching the movement prepositions; classroom rules judged true/false and two rules written from word tiles (*Do not eat in class*); a second phonics page on blends (cl, cr, pl, st, sl, gr, sw); a contractions lesson ("Let's check: do not = don't. Can you write the short word?" — I'm, he's, isn't, there's, can't, what's); Is there a …? / Are there any …? guessing game; adjective–noun matching (a big house); this/that/these/those sentence building with comparatives (*This balloon is bigger. These rulers are longer*); tracing and matching numbers 10–100; the class timetable (*What's on Tuesday?*); Whose …? matching and answers (*Whose hat is it? — It's Lina's*); the partner shop game with top/middle/bottom shelves; a numbers listening page (11–100 in words and figures); spelling grid: here, there, than, whose; and story work on *The Lion and the Mouse* (sentence completion, "Who said …?").

**Unit 2 (pp. 20–35).** *Where's he from? / Where are they from?* with the "Remember" box for he's/she's/they're from; a capital-letters lesson (*These names have capital letters* — countries, people, languages; write monday, arabic, friday, china, kareem correctly); completing the Adel–Sam conversation and writing about yourself; an Activity-Book-only information file on **Jordan** (capital Amman, Arabic, mild and dry) with question–answer writing; the Iraq–Russia comparison file with Yes/No checking; ordering numbers to 1000 and a number-crossing game; a "th" phonics page (bathroom, thousand, three, think, thumb, teeth); a school-day model text by Yousif with a draw-and-write version of your own; writing clock times; the "Can you help me?" challenge game; landscape matching with the compass points (north, south, east, west); adjective gap-fill; reading puzzles to guess (mountain, desert, river, city) and writing your own puzzle; geography anagrams; spelling grid: from, about, that, say; odd-one-out and word-group sorting; story work on *City Mouse and Village Mouse* (city/village vocabulary sort).

**Unit 3 (pp. 36–51).** Cloze of Lina's Open Day text; word groups (numbers/people/countries); completing the months rhyme and the birthday conversation (*When is your birthday? — It's in July*); the alphabet and alphabet order (say the alphabet, fill the gaps, put food words in dictionary order); "The Yummy Stall" menu with IQD prices — listening choices, writing your order and totalling the bill; the alphabet-order challenge (partners order each other's words); correcting misspellings (cokies, mlik, piza, wata, tee, swets); a read-cover-and-remember text about the races with comprehension questions; Whose is this? with clothes and jumbled-sentence writing (*It is Yousif's shirt*); a phonics page on sh, ch, ph (shop, chair, elephant, shirt, cheese, Euphrates, phone, photo …); "Looking at syllables" — clapping and sorting words into 1, 2 and 3 syllables; missing-letter sentences from the "What's wrong?" picture; a numbers listening game; ordinal balloons (1st–4th) and writing results sentences from a test-marks table (*Yousif was first in Arabic*); completing the Snowy Mountain School poster; helping Adel write his Race Day email; spelling grid: our, your, very, want; adverb hunt in the *Sports Races* story (shouted loudly, walked slowly …) and sentence completion with -ly adverbs; and a pronunciation page distinguishing -teen from -ty numbers (thirteen/thirty, fourteen/forty …).

**Unit 4 (pp. 52–55).** Revision: pupils write their own version of the "Bigger and smaller" rhyme from word banks; "Check your progress!" — a five-part self-test (cloze letter, word-order rules with full stops, personal questions, adjective matching, listening check); Kareem's "About Iraq" homework text printed full of capital-letter mistakes (iraq, baghdad, arabic, march, basra circled in the model) to find and correct; and an Activity-Book-only story, *The wrong shoes*, a present-tense narrative about Sam wearing his dad's shoes to school, with speech-bubble ordering.

**Unit 5 (pp. 56–71).** Adjective gap-fill and spelling dialogues (*How do you spell ancient?*); definition-guessing in pairs (museum, bracelet, cave, forest, boat, pencil, waterfall, ruin); fruit-preference tables and drawing a bar chart; a grammar box on irregular comparison — **"Good, better and best"** (*Strawberry ice-cream is better. But chocolate ice-cream is the best!*) — with chart questions using *better than* and *best*; a *going to* seaside text and picture vocabulary (boat, sandals, umbrella, sunglasses, plane …); completing the *going to* holiday conversation; the zoo (animals matching, the zoo timetable with ordinal activities, the zoo shop price list to total in IQD); a "Let's learn!" text on the Nile (*The Nile is the longest river in the world. It is about 6,650 kilometres long*) with anagrams (Nile, Africa, Egypt) and river gap-fill (streams, waterfalls, float); river questions (*How long is the river Tigris (dijlah)?*); superlative syllable stress (biggest, longest, hottest …); the "Around the world" / "Animals around the world" superlative texts (longest crocodile, hottest place in Iraq — Basra 57°C, oldest elephant, biggest shark, smallest bird, hottest desert); superlative anagrams and odd-one-out; word groups; spelling grid: now, many, much, their; story ordering for *Rami and the School Trip*; and a phonics page on ee, ea and y making the same sound (sea, see, cheese, teacher, happy, bee), with the warning that some ea words sound different (breakfast, wear, ear).

**Unit 6 (pp. 72–87).** Question-word choice (including *Whose* and *How old*) with a punctuation page (match capital letters / full stop / question mark; "A capital letter starts a sentence. A full stop ends a sentence"); matching answers to questions; the days-of-the-week table (*What day was it yesterday? — It was …; What day will it be tomorrow? — It will be …*) and a days rhyme to complete; matching "Yesterday, I …" past sentences to pictures; the -ed pronunciation sort — pupils hear and file regular past verbs under /d/, /t/ and /ɪd/ (played, washed, visited …); a LOOK box stating the regular rule (*These are verbs in the past. They are regular verbs — they end in -ed. play + ed = played, live + d = lived*) with choose-the-correct-verb sentences; an irregular-verbs lesson built on a joke ("Can we say 'goed'? Yesterday, I goed to school." — "No, we can't! Because this verb is irregular. We say 'went'.") and classifying 18 verb pairs as regular or irregular (go/went, am/are–was/were, have/had, get/got, see/saw, make/made, do/did, eat/ate, swim/swam, buy/bought, learn/learnt, give/gave, draw/drew, hear/heard, say/said …); completing Liz's past-tense story; finding which parts of a sentence answer What/Where/Who/When and writing the missing *did* question words; a past-tense board game ("Congratulations! You know your past tense!"); a wordsearch of past forms; Nadia's weekend email to complete with past verbs and then correct false sentences about it; "Lina's day" true-item counting; Iraq weather gap-fill; spelling grid: why, when, was, were; word-category boxes (countries, sports/games, places, regular past verbs, irregular past verbs, adjectives); and story work on *The Little Brown Bird* with a "Let's check!" box explaining nouns and adjectives (*Nouns are the names of things. Adjectives tell us about the things*).

**Unit 7 (pp. 88–103).** Completing the Red River School challenge letter (multiple-choice cloze); writing the monsters' questions from their answers; alphabet-order revision; packing-needs matching (*You are going to go to the seaside … What do you need? — I need some sandals and my swimming things*); completing the months from their endings; a phonics page on -igh and y sounding like "I" (high, night, light, butterfly, cry, sky, why); numbers in words to 2,000 in a number puzzle; ordering the *Have you got …?* borrowing conversations (*Have you got a football? — No, I haven't, but I need one for school today. Can I borrow yours?*); the school library rules — reading *Do not …* rules and rewriting them with must/mustn't (*The first one says we mustn't shout*), with a "Let's check! must not = mustn't" box; a project dialogue about making flags for the School Challenge (Iraqi flag, Green Tree School flag, Red River School flag) and flag-making instructions; judging the maths/art/English challenge rules against the Pupil's Book pages; unscrambling Unit 7 sentences with attention to capital letters; an opinion scale (✓✓✓✓ VERY … ✗ NO) on must/mustn't statements; rewriting the library rules as *You mustn't shout in the library*; spelling grid: must, mustn't, any, need; and comprehension questions on *The Big Bike Race*.

**Unit 8 (pp. 104–108) and appendices (unnumbered, PDF pp. 109–120).** Revision unit: spelling the months from letter chunks and writing correctly punctuated sentences (*when is your birthday → When is your birthday?*); "Check my progress!" — a cloze letter about the School Challenge and a past-tense reading text ("Read about you") answered with did-questions; the "I can say it in English!" board game (26 prompts sampling the whole book); *The Goodbye Song* as a cloze (a sung summary of Grade 4: comparatives, numbers, past of do/eat, must/mustn't); and blank Spelling tests grids for Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7. The book closes with cut-out word cards (PDF pp. 109–112, first letters printed red), cut-out picture cards (PDF pp. 113–116) and Game circles 1–4 (PDF pp. 117–120, bound in the order 3, 1, 2, 4).

### Grammar spine

The workbook practises the Pupil's Book spine — present continuous, comparatives and superlatives, there is/are and there was/were, possessive 's, present simple and time, going to, past simple, have got with some/any, must/mustn't — and adds the course's explicit grammar and mechanics teaching, which lives here rather than in the Pupil's Book: contractions (I'm, don't, isn't, can't); capital letters (names, countries, languages, days, months) and sentence punctuation (full stop, question mark); the regular -ed rule and the regular/irregular distinction with a working irregular list; irregular comparison good/better/best; nouns vs adjectives; must not = mustn't; and a full phonics strand — initial blends, th, sh/ch/ph, syllables, -teen/-ty stress, ee/ea/y, -igh/y, and the three pronunciations of -ed — plus dictionary skills (alphabet order) and per-unit spelling grids.

### Honest negatives

- **A workbook, not a second coursebook**: with a handful of exceptions (the Jordan information file, the zoo spread, the Nile text, *The wrong shoes*, *The Goodbye Song*), it introduces no new language points of its own — it practises, spells and tests the Pupil's Book's syllabus, and it cannot be used without the Pupil's Book and the audio (many pages are listen-and-write).
- **No answer key** is printed in the book; checking is delegated to the audio ("Listen and check your answers") and the teacher.
- **No Arabic** anywhere in this book — the English–Arabic support lives in the Pupil's Book's "Your Dictionary"; the Activity Book is English-only, including its grammar boxes.
- Copy/scan notes: the file is complete (120/120 pages). The interior is greyscale except the colour cut-out card pages; the cover in this copy is a greyscale scan without the "Activity Book" label (see identity verdict). The game circles at the back are bound in scrambled order (3, 1, 2, 4), and Kareem's "About Iraq" text (p. 54) is printed with deliberate capitalisation errors as a correction exercise — as printed, not a defect.

## Grade 4 — English for Iraq 4th Primary Teacher's Book (teacher's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH in substance, with the series' own naming. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Teacher's Guide"; the book itself is titled **Teacher's Book** on its cover.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 4th Primary — Teacher's Book* (front cover).
- **Authors as printed:** **Sarah MacBurnie and Sarah Curtis** (cover). The imprint asserts the rights of "the authors" jointly. The imprint thanks Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Specialist) and Hala Talib Hammood (Trainer and Co-ordinator to the English Curriculum Committee) as the primary specialists who supported the Editorial and Adaptation Committee. Note: the Grade 4 Pupil's and Activity Books credit MacBurnie alone as author — Curtis is credited on this Teacher's Book only.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (imprint); cover: Garnet Education; issued for the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition, copyright © 2016 Garnet Publishing Ltd." (imprint). Production code: IRQ16 TB 4thP.
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed anywhere in the book.
- **PDF pages:** 207, all accounted for (see method note in Honest negatives).
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** Printed page = PDF page through the front matter and Unit 1 (the contents page is PDF p. 3, the Introduction starts at printed p. 5, Teaching Notes Unit 1 at printed p. 33 = PDF p. 33). By the appendices the printed folios run **one ahead** of the PDF pages (Spelling Grids printed p. 200 = PDF p. 199; the final Notes page is printed p. 208 = PDF p. 207): somewhere in the middle the printed numbering gains a page (a folio slip inside the printed book, or one unfoliated leaf in this scan). No content is missing — contents, all eight units of notes and all four appendices are present and in order. All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text-layer character:** None — image-only greyscale scan. Read from 110-DPI 2-up contact sheets (front matter, Book Map, all unit front pages and all appendices in full; lesson notes read across every unit), with a RapidOCR pass over the renders as a search index. No scanner wrapper.
- **Design:** Bilingual throughout — every English page is mirrored by an Arabic translation page (contents included), so an Arabic-speaking teacher can follow the whole book in Arabic. The Grade 4 book is the most literacy-focused of the three primary teacher's books: it adds a **spelling-test routine** (each unit's Lesson 1 opens by dictating the previous unit's four high-frequency words into the Activity Book's spelling grid), a handwriting/cursive strand in the Introduction, and a fourth appendix, the **List of Picture/Word Cards** (86 numbered cards), reflecting the course's card-based vocabulary teaching at this level. Components listed: Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Your Dictionary (English–Arabic), Pupil's mini Picture/Word Cards, Teacher's Book, Teacher Resource File audio, and nine A2 posters.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Introduction (pp. 5–31).** The bilingual front matter follows the series pattern (Key features, Components, The course, Aims, Advice to teachers, Teaching the course, Book Map) with Grade 4 specifics. The course "has eight units, comprising 56 lessons in total"; Units 4 and 8 are shorter revision units of four lessons, and "The story comes at the end of every unit in Lesson 8 (Lesson 3 in Units 4 and 8)". Key features stress "Revision of sound-letter correlation, consonant blends (br, pl) and digraphs (sh, ph)", "Lots of guided writing practice" and the A5 Picture/Word Cards. The cast is named: "Yousif and his younger sister Lina, their school friends Kareem and Nadia, Rana and Tariq". The advice section is calibrated to nine-year-olds ("Characteristics of nine-year-olds") and to literacy: three reading approaches (whole-word recognition and high-frequency words; a phonic approach; audio-assisted reading), a named section on high-frequency words ("In Units 1–3 and 5–7, pupils learn to spell common high-frequency words using the 'Look, say, cover, write, check' strategy. Pupils learn four words in each unit. Lesson 1 of the following unit includes a spelling test"), reading beyond the sentence level, reading aloud, and a writing section that follows letter formation through to joined writing — pupils "continue to master the first steps towards faster, confident cursive script by learning to end each letter with a 'flick' stroke". The Book Map (pp. 30–31) gives each unit's aims and key language (reproduced in the Pupil's Book filing).

**Unit 1 Teaching Notes (pp. 33–75).** The unit front page sets out Aims by skill (Listening / Speaking / Reading / Writing) and the Language and Word list (playground, school shop, prepositions up/down/through/along/around, comparatives, sequencers, positioning, and the spelling words here, there, than, whose). Each lesson then follows the book's fixed pattern: an Aims/Materials box (PB and AB pages, track numbers, extra materials), a Teaching note where needed, Warm up, numbered Stages mixing Pupil's Book and Activity Book work, full audio transcripts printed inside the notes, printed Answers, Homework setting, Rounding off, and an "Additional" box with an Extend activity and a Support activity. Lesson 1's teaching notes handle *favourite* against *like … best* and Grade 4 punctuation ("in activities like the one at the bottom of Activity Book page 5, full stops are not given at the end of any 'write-on' lines"); Lesson 3's Extend is the Pelmanism card game; Lesson 4's teaching note explains contractions ("Use the Let's check box … to elicit what has happened to make don't out of do not. Show pupils how the vowel has gone and has been replaced by an apostrophe", and for *cannot/can't* that "can not loses n as well as the vowel o"); Lesson 6's note separates the two apostrophes in *It's Lina's* — "In It's, the 's indicates a contracted (shortened) form … In Lina's, the 's indicates possession or ownership".

**Unit 2 Teaching Notes (pp. 76–95).** Countries and capitals. Lesson 1 opens with the Unit 1 spelling test (here, there, than, whose dictated into the Activity Book p. 108 grid) and a teaching note on the world map and continents ("It is probably best just to refer to the continents in passing and, as an interesting fact, show them that Iraq is in Western Asia in the area generally called the Middle East"). The notes run the capital-letter rule explicitly ("in English it is very important to use capital letters when writing names of people, countries, cities, etc.") and drill the contractions in the *Where's he from?* exchanges.

**Unit 3 Teaching Notes (pp. 96–117).** Open Day, months, food and clothes. Lesson 1's teaching note explains the British school open day and the word *stall* for Iraqi classrooms; the spelling test covers Unit 2's words (from, about, that, say). The notes handle the months rhyme, the can-requests, prices in IQD, possessive 's and the sh/ch/ph clusters per the Book Map aims.

**Unit 4 Teaching Notes (pp. 118–127).** Revision of Units 1–3 in four lessons: the team challenge with its Team A/Team B question sets printed in the notes, writing a rhyme from the Unit 3 model, the can-request/price dialogue (with a note on *How much is the …?* for uncountables and containers), and the Activity Book "Check my progress!" page talked through exercise by exercise.

**Unit 5 Teaching Notes (pp. 128–149).** Places, going to and superlatives. Lesson 1 works the fact file and the definition-matching, noting that Track 74 gives "one possible set of matches for the nouns and adjectives" and that other combinations are acceptable — the notes repeatedly licence teacher judgement of this kind. Pelmanism returns as the Extend game with place nouns and adjectives.

**Unit 6 Teaching Notes (pp. 150–169).** Wh- questions and the past. Lesson 1's teaching note is phonetic: "the wh- prefix can make two different sounds. In what, where, when and why it makes a /w/ sound; but in who and whose, it makes a /h/ sound", with the silent h in who/whose drilled on Track 102; it also notes *Whose* is revised in the Activity Book because it was already taught in Unit 1 Lesson 6. The unit's remaining notes cover the instructions lesson, was/were, the regular/irregular past work and the email, per the Book Map.

**Unit 7 Teaching Notes (pp. 170–189).** The school challenge, needs and must/mustn't. Lesson 1 reviews Wh- questions and ordinals, with a written/spoken date note ("Dates are written as, for example, Tuesday, 4th April, but when we say a date, we add the and of as here in 'Tuesday, the 4th of April'"), the spelling test on Unit 6's words (why, when, was, were), and the question-monster Activity Book work.

**Unit 8 Teaching Notes (pp. 190–197).** Revision of Units 5–7 in four lessons, closing with a pupil-devised spelling quiz ("Pupils have practised 24 words in 'Look, say, cover, write, check' so select ten words, some quite easy and some more difficult") and an Extend/Support task in which groups write their own eight-question quiz from the whole book and swap.

**Appendices (pp. 198–208).** The **Teacher Resource File Transcript** (pp. 198–199) prints Tracks 152–158: the key language of Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 (the revision units have none) and Track 158, "Useful classroom language" — the full phrase bank (praise, instructions, lesson management, question forms, farewells) that the Introduction tells teachers to display on the classroom wall. Then the photocopiable **Spelling Grids** (p. 200, matching Activity Book p. 108), **Game Circles** 1–4 (pp. 201–204, one per page), the **List of Picture/Word Cards** (p. 205) — 86 numbered cards from *along* to *young*, including teaching items like *full stop*, *question mark* and *vowels (a, e, i, o, u)* — and blank Notes pages (pp. 206–208).

### Grammar spine

The Teacher's Book teaches the Grade 4 grammar implicitly, "as formulaic chunks" — its own Introduction states the language is functional and "the grammar is implicit, i.e., there is no focus on abstract grammatical rules and patterns". What the notes add beyond the Pupil's Book is the metalanguage for the teacher: the apostrophe's two jobs (contraction vs possession), cannot → can't letter loss, the /w/ vs /h/ sounds of wh-, capital letters for proper nouns, full stops and question marks as a taught punctuation aim, and the high-frequency-word spelling system. The pupil-facing grammar sequence itself is the Pupil's Book's (see the Grade 4 Pupil's Book filing): comparatives and *Whose …?* belong to Unit 1, going to and superlatives to Unit 5, past simple to Unit 6, must/mustn't and have got to Unit 7.

### Honest negatives

- **No answer key section and no tests**: answers live inside the lesson notes; assessment is the spelling-test routine plus observation guidance ("How do teachers assess learning?" — observe, listen, collect Activity Book work, self-assessment). There is no equivalent of the Grade 6 book's fuller assessment discussion.
- **No new pupil language**: a teacher's book — it adds transcripts, answers, procedures and pedagogy only.
- Reading-method note (stated plainly): the front matter (pp. 1–32), the whole of Unit 1's notes (pp. 33–75), every unit's front page and Lesson 1 spread, further lesson spreads in Units 4, 5 and 8, and all appendices (pp. 198–208) were read in full from the contact sheets; the remaining lesson-procedure pages follow the identical bilingual template verified across all eight units and were read at their openings and through the OCR index rather than line by line. The Arabic pages are translations of the English pages throughout (verified on the contents and on spreads in every unit).
- Copy/scan notes: greyscale scan, complete; the printed foliation gains one page on the PDF count by the appendices (see identity block) — a numbering slip, not missing content. No back cover is bound in this file (it ends at the blank Notes p. 208). No ISBN is printed.

### Identity block

- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 5th Primary — Pupil's Book* (front cover, PDF p. 1).
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie and Sally Rabi (cover). The imprint (PDF p. 2) asserts the copyright right for Sarah MacBurnie alone. The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha (Senior Education Specialist), Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid (Senior Education Specialist), Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Supervisor), Hanaa Adil Ali (Teacher of English). A Book Map (printed pp. 3–5) and an English–Arabic "Your Dictionary" (pp. 110–120) frame the book.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 (pp. 6–21) — Countries and nationalities.** A world map of eight countries (Thailand, France, Japan, Egypt, England, Iraq, Turkey, Italy) with *I'm from Iraq. Where are you from?*, flags and short answers with *be* set out in a "Let's check" table (*Is Dima from England? — No, she isn't. She's from Iraq. Are Murad and Ali from Turkey? — Yes, they are.*). Lesson 3, "My nationality", adds the nationality adjectives (Egyptian, Italian, Iraqi, English, Turkish, Japanese, Thai, French) and *What nationality are you? — I'm Iraqi*. Lesson 4, "Where's it from?", is a class survey about where foods and animals come from (kleicha, macaroni, pandas, elephants, curry, masgouf, kangaroos). A project page prints a class text about food with the nationality words deliberately wrong (kleicha called a "Thai cookie", curry "from France", pandas "from Italy") for pupils to spot and rewrite. Lesson 5, "Learning English", teaches *I want to learn English because …* with reasons (study in English, books and songs, travel, the internet). Lessons 6–7 are "My Magazine: Country information file" — photo texts about England (Jake: London, Tower Bridge, the London Eye, the Queen's palace) and India (Tanvi: Mumbai, New Delhi, the Qutub minaret, the Taj Mahal), a reading text about Iraq (Baghdad, Arabic and Kurdish, the Tigris and Euphrates, Babylon, the Marshes, Malwiya Minaret, the Zagros mountains, Bekhal waterfall), Yes/No checking and a Lebanon information file to complete. Lesson 8 is the rhyme "Let's be friends", and a board game closes the unit.

**Unit 2 (pp. 22–37) — Time, routines and animals.** Times said in words (7.45 *seven forty-five*, 12.00 *twelve o'clock*, 9.30 *nine thirty*) with digital clocks, and a daily-times survey. "Mr Hadi's day" presents the zookeeper's routine in the third person (*Mr Hadi gets up at six thirty. He feeds the animals at eleven fifteen*), with a "Let's check" box contrasting the I/you/we/they form against he/she/it + -s, and *has* for breakfast. "Things we do every day" (clean, wash clothes, cook, watch TV, help my children, see friends, go shopping) is practised with *What does she do on Tuesday?*, *Does she clean the house on Thursday? — Yes, she does / No, she doesn't*, and *When does he feed the animals?*. Lessons 4–5, "What's it like?" and "Thinking about animals", teach character adjectives for animals sorted into positive and negative (dangerous, greedy, friendly, playful, gentle, lazy, intelligent, social), the he/she-for-people vs it-for-animals "Let's check", and *I think … are dangerous*. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Insects in our life" — short science texts on butterflies (egg → caterpillar → adult), ants (colony, queen/worker/soldier ants, lifting 20 times their body weight) and flies (two wings, carrying disease). Lesson 7, "Check my understanding", corrects false sentences about the texts. The unit ends with a Dove project poster and the song "What are they like?".

**Unit 3 (pp. 38–53) — Sports and how often.** Ten sports (tennis, boxing, running, karate, cycling, swimming, football, gymnastics, rowing, basketball) with *What's your favourite sport?*. Lesson 2, "Play or go or do?", states the collocation rules in a "Let's check" box: *play* for games and ball games, *go* for -ing activities (except *do boxing*), *do* for non-team activities like karate. Lesson 3, "How often do you …?", introduces the frequency scale always (100) – often (75) – sometimes (50) – never (0) and *How often do you clean your teeth? — I always clean my teeth after breakfast*. Lesson 4 covers weekend activities (see a film, go to a museum, play with friends, help at home, play in the park, have a picnic). Lesson 5, "A class survey", adds *once a day, twice a week, three times a month, four times a year* ("Let's check"), and Lesson 5's second half reads a bar chart with the survey words *all the pupils, most pupils, only 8 pupils, everybody*. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Sports are great" (why we do sports: keep healthy, make friends, learn rules, have fun, play outside), Lesson 7 checks understanding of it, and the project is a Volleyball poster. The Lesson 8 story, *Our sports club*, mixes present narration with *going to* plans (*I am going to play tennis and do karate*).

**Unit 4 (pp. 54–57) — Revision of Units 1–3.** Four lessons: the two-team class challenge (nationalities, times in numbers, go/goes and have/has choices, frequency adverbs); "My progress" with a factual reading text, *Camels* (Arabian and Asian camels, humps, drinking 135 litres in one go), answered in writing; "An email to a friend" — Firas's email about the sports club printed with ten wrong words (arts→sports, sad→excited, small→big, field→pool, never→always, once a month→three times a week, do tennis→play, play karate→do, sweets→a hat, football→a tennis racket) to correct; and a number-circle language game.

**Unit 5 (pp. 58–73) — Food, picnics and health.** Food and drink words (water, bread, fish, milk, strawberries, chicken, rice, apples, cookies, meat, cherries, salad, cucumber, onion, potato, tomato). Lesson 2, "What can you count?", teaches countable vs uncountable nouns with the "Let's check" question table (*Are there any + countable? — Yes, there are / No, there aren't. Is there any + uncountable? — Yes, there is / No, there isn't*), containers (tin, box, cup, bottle, packet, bag — *a box of chocolates, a tin of lemonade*), and quantities (*Yes, there are lots. Yes, there is a lot. There aren't any strawberries.*). Lesson 3, "Would you like …?", is a picnic scene: *Would you like some salad? — Yes, please. / No, thank you. I'd prefer tea, please. Would you like tea or lemonade?*, with a follow-up invitation email to read and answer. Lesson 4, "Are you healthy?", is a class health survey (fruit and vegetables, sleep 9 hours, water, exercise, sweets, TV/computer games). Lesson 5 compares two lunchboxes. Lesson 6 is the magazine story *Picnic by the lake* — a family picnic told in the present tense with past-time sequencing — followed in Lesson 7 by sentence-ordering, retelling and writing your own "My Picnic" story from a frame. Lesson 8 is the rhyme "Grapes and apples" and a plate-guessing game (*Is there any salad on this plate?*).

**Unit 6 (pp. 74–89) — In the city.** Shops (clothes shop, butcher's, shoe shop, pharmacy, fruit and vegetable stall, bank, toy shop, bakery) and *Where can you get meat from?*. Lesson 2, "Excuse me. Where's …?", teaches directions with behind, next to, opposite, between on a street map (*Excuse me. I'm lost. Where's the shoe shop? — It's on Green Street between the clothes shop and the bakery*), plus *I want to buy some bread. — Then go to the baker on Green Street*. Lesson 3, "Helping Mum", anchors *was/were* in a shopping trip (*At 10.30, Mum was at the pharmacy. At 10.45, Rahaf and Ameer were at the toy shop*), with a "Let's check" on long and short answers and the drill *Where were you at nine o'clock yesterday morning? — I was at school*. Lesson 4, "At Fun City", reads about a holiday in Erbil and names the rides (roller coaster, bumper cars, merry-go-round, super slide, karting, big wheel, super swing, clowns), with *Would you like to go on the big wheel? — Yes, I would. / No, I wouldn't. It's too fast*. Lesson 5, "Souvenirs", identifies souvenirs from London and Paris (postcard, mug, T-shirt, key ring, baseball cap, doll). Lesson 6, "My Magazine: A fun day out", reads three attraction adverts (Fun City, Water World, Magic Castles — with opening days and IQD prices), Lesson 7 checks understanding, plans a new fun place and reads Firas's postcard from Baghdad (the big wheel in Al-Zawraa Park, a boat on the Tigris). The project is a fun-day-out poster and Lesson 8 is "The city song" (*My favourite place!*).

**Unit 7 (pp. 90–105) — Seasons, weather and holidays.** The four seasons with their months and typical weather (*What's the weather like in spring? — It's cool, rainy and mild*). Lesson 2, "Going on a summer holiday", presents *going to* for holiday plans with the full paradigm in a "Let's check" box (I'm/you're/he's/we're/they're going to + verb), by transport in questions, and *It's going to be hot and sunny*. Lesson 3, "At the airport", sets a flight board (Frankfurt, Istanbul, Amman, Beirut, Cairo, Doha, Singapore, Dublin) and teaches possessive pronouns through lost luggage (*Whose big black bag is this? — It's mine. The red bag is mine. Mine is green. My teddy's bag is blue and white*), completed next lesson by my/your/her/his + noun against mine/yours/hers/his (*It's her pencil case. It's hers. They're his*). Lesson 4, "Presents for everyone", practises *I'm going to give the key ring to my grandmother* and gift-receiving conversations (*I bought you some flowers. — Flowers! They're lovely!*). Lesson 5, "Holiday weather", is a past-tense photo narration of Noor's Egypt holiday (*We went to the beach. It was sunny and very hot. I learnt to row a boat. We didn't take any photos because it was windy. I rode on a camel. We flew back home*) followed by three short holiday recount texts (Basra, the marshes, the northern mountains) for talking about past holidays. Lesson 6, "My School Magazine: How we travel", collects four short travel texts (flying to Annajaf Al-Ashraf, driving to Diyala, a school ferryboat picnic, a first train ride to Nasiriyah) with a "Remember" box: *go by car/bus/train/boat/plane* but *go on foot*. The project is a "Favourite holiday" poster (a Jordan holiday: Petra, mansaf, floating in the Dead Sea). Lesson 8 is the story *Karam's dream* — a dream trip to France told in the present continuous inside a past frame.

**Unit 8 (pp. 106–109) — Revision of Units 5–7.** Four lessons: the class challenge (some/any, would-like offers, shop prepositions, *Where were you …?*, her/hers and my/mine choices, holiday questions); "An email to a friend" — Rayan's Egypt holiday email with nine present-tense verbs printed in blue to rewrite in the past (is→was, fly→flew, doesn't→didn't, do→did, see→saw, likes→liked, buy→bought, take→took); "Rayan's holiday quiz" (describing his holiday photos for an email reply); and the closing song "Our Goodbye Party".

**Your Dictionary (pp. 110–120).** An English–Arabic wordlist by unit for all eight units (Unit Four contributes the camel text's words — calf, collect, eyelashes, hairy, hump, in one go, protect, task, thick — and Unit Eight just exciting, party, race).

### Grammar spine

The year is built on the present simple, taught as a system: third-person -s with a full "Let's check" paradigm (Unit 2), do/does questions and doesn't negatives, times said in words, and the frequency adverbs always/often/sometimes/never plus once/twice/three times a week (Unit 3). Around it: *be* questions and nationalities (Unit 1); play/go/do collocations (Unit 3); countable/uncountable nouns with some/any, a lot/lots and containers (Unit 5); *Would you like …? — I'd like / I'd prefer* (Units 5–6, with would/wouldn't answers); the past of *be* — was/were — with clock times (Unit 6); *going to* for plans with its paradigm box (Unit 7); possessive pronouns mine/yours/his/hers against possessive adjectives (Unit 7); prepositions of place behind/next to/opposite/between (Unit 6) and *by* + transport with *on foot* (Unit 7). The lexical past simple (went, flew, rode, learnt, bought, took, saw) is carried by the reading texts and only corrected as a system in the Unit 8 revision email.

### Honest negatives

- **No present perfect, no passive, no conditionals, no reported speech.** The present continuous is never taught as a point in this book — it occurs inside story narration (*Dad is driving, Mum is next to Dad*) and in *Karam's dream*, but there is no -ing lesson or paradigm.
- **The lexical past simple is used long before it is systematised**: holiday recounts in Unit 7 (including *didn't* in "We didn't take any photos") presuppose went/flew/rode/learnt; the only explicit treatment is the Unit 8 email-correction exercise. There are no past-tense tables and no did-questions in the teaching lessons (one appears in the Unit 8 challenge: *What did you do on your holiday?*).
- **Comparison is recycled, not taught**: comparatives from Grade 4 appear inside texts (*Mumbai is bigger than New Delhi*), but this book has no comparative or superlative lesson of its own.
- **No phonics or spelling strand in the Pupil's Book** (that work sits in the Activity Book); handwriting and punctuation are practised only through the email/correction tasks.
- Copy/scan notes: this copy is complete and clean (120/120 pages in order). The Unit 1 project text (p. 13) and the Unit 4/Unit 8 emails are printed with deliberate errors as correction exercises, as designed. No ISBN is printed anywhere in this copy.

## Grade 5 — English for Iraq 5th Primary Activity Book (activity book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Activity Book"; the cover prints "Activity Book" and the imprint production code (IRQ17 **AB** 5thP) confirms the Grade 5 Activity Book.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 5th Primary — Activity Book* (front cover).
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie and Andrew Hornsby-Smith (cover and imprint — the imprint asserts both authors' rights). The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha, Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid (Senior Education Specialists), Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Supervisor), Hanaa Adil Ali (Teacher of English).
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (imprint); cover: Garnet Education "For Iraq", Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition © 2017 Garnet Publishing Ltd." (imprint). Production code: IRQ17 AB 5thP. The back cover is dated 2017 and lists the course components (Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Teacher's Book, Audio, Posters).
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed. The back cover carries a publisher's barcode with the product code TRG003807 — a stock code, not an ISBN.
- **PDF pages:** 112, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** This file binds **two scans of the front cover** (PDF p. 1 in colour, PDF p. 2 in greyscale), then the imprint (PDF p. 3) and the "This book belongs to" page (PDF p. 4). From the first workbook page (Unit 1 Lesson 1, printed p. 4, at PDF p. 5), **printed page = PDF page − 1** for the rest of the book: the printed folios run 4–110 (Spelling tests p. 108, Game circle 1 p. 109, Game circle 2 p. 110), and PDF p. 112 is the back cover. All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text-layer character:** None — image-only scan (greyscale interior). Read whole from 100-DPI 4-up contact sheets, with a RapidOCR pass over the renders as a search index. A round third-party sticker logo (an Iraq-map roundel with Arabic text) is overlaid on a few scanned pages (e.g. printed pp. 11, 84, 109) — a mark of this scan copy, not part of the printed book.
- **Cast/design:** Black-and-white workbook organised by lettered exercises (A, B, C …) for every Pupil's Book lesson, with three signature devices: **LOOK boxes** that state the grammar and spelling rules the Pupil's Book only models, **"I CAN …" / "MY NEW WORDS"** self-assessment pages closing each unit, and a per-unit "Look, say, cover, write, check" spelling grid. It also carries reading texts of its own (India, Oman, dolphins, a health-survey pair, Sardar's city trip, Jena's Erbil notebook).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 (pp. 4–19).** Write-about-yourself from Dima's model (*I'm Dima. I'm from Iraq. I live in Basra*); question–answer matching; missing letters in country names; numbering countries in alphabetical order; completing *be* questions with is/isn't/are/aren't; flag–child matching; a LOOK box of the *be* forms (*I'm from Iraq. He's/She's from Iraq. We're/They're from Iraq*); correcting false origin sentences in two steps (*She's from Egypt → She isn't from Egypt. She's from Iraq*); unscrambling country names with capital letters; nationality sentence writing; a LOOK box on nationality endings (-i, -ian, -ish, -ese) with the warning "Some nationalities are irregular" (Thailand → Thai, France → French); food information files (masgouf, macaroni, kleicha, curry) and a countries crossword; the Pupil's Book "Learning English" sentences completed and extended (*I want to … because …* matched and written about yourself); an Activity-Book-only reading text about **India** (New Delhi and Mumbai, Hindi/Tamil/Urdu, the tiger as national symbol, saris, the Himalayas, the monsoon) with cover-and-recall completion; an Activity-Book-only text about **Oman** (Muscat, a 1,700 km coastline, castles, Arabian horses, kuma hats) with an information file to complete; mistake-hunting sentences (*They is from Iraq. The capital of Iraq are Baghdad. People from Iraq are Iraqian.*); spelling grid: which, isn't, aren't, wrong; and the "I CAN — Name countries and nationalities" page with a capital-letters review (names of countries, people and nationalities), questions about Iraq, "My new words" and country riddles.

**Unit 2 (pp. 20–35).** Writing times in words onto clock faces and correcting time sentences against pictures; "Let's say it and remember" — underlining the strong part of -teen vs -ty numbers (thirteen/thirty …); an Activity-Book-only story about Jake's Saturday told in the third person, with a hunt for all the -s verbs sorted into I and he/she/it columns; verb–object matching (wash clothes, cook dinner, watch TV, help with homework …); a LOOK box tabulating Yes/No and Wh- questions with do/does; unscrambling routine sentences with capital letters; the sounds of the verb ending -s sorted into /s/, /z/ and /ɪz/ (helps, cleans, washes …); building the animal adjectives from their suffixes (play-ful, beauti-ful, danger-ous, greed-y, laz-y, intellig-ent, friend-ly, gent-le) and using them in sentences; alphabet-order work (missing letters, ordering eleven words, judging given orders); a cloze text "About dolphins" (friendly, live, playful, intelligent, family) with Pupil A / Pupil B question sheets (dolphins vs horses); animal naming and spelling-check conversations (*How do you spell donkey? — d-o-n-k-e-y*); insect description riddles (ladybird, bee, spider — the spider text noting spiders are not insects); writing interesting insect facts (*I think bees are interesting because …*); a partner quiz; mistake sentences with exactly two errors each (*Most ants lives together on a colony*); spelling grid: two, eight, does, goes; and the "I CAN — 'What's it like?'" page: sorting adjectives positive/negative and correcting a shark homework text using teacher's correction symbols (V = verb, SP = spelling, C = capital letter, WO = word order, WW = wrong word), plus animal riddles.

**Unit 3 (pp. 36–51).** A sports crossword with a mystery word and anagrams; a sports rhyme to complete; a LOOK box on *and/or* with negatives (*I like football and tennis. I don't like football or tennis. don't = do not*); rewriting wrong-verb sentences (*I play gymnastics → I do gymnastics*) and sorting activities under do/go/play; completing conversations and reading Class B's survey table to write questions; completing the frequency arrow (100/75/50/0); days of the week in order (starting Sunday); phrases with in/on/at/with (*at the weekend, in bed, on Saturday, at six o'clock*); Kamal's routine text as a listen-and-correct task; phrase building from two boxes; writing the long forms of contractions (don't, doesn't, what's, where's, we're, he's); once/twice/three-times phrases and a "Let's remember" time puzzle (24 hours in a day … 365 days in a year); anagrams of play/go/do phrases and vowel-gap word completion; a Dima–Firas conversation to colour by listening; reading a pie chart of Class C's TV habits; an Activity-Book-only TV guide (Tuesday/Wednesday programmes — *Learn to paint*, *Let's visit Baghdad's new sports club*, *Football: Al-Zawraa X Al-Shorta*) with *What are you going to watch on Tuesday?* conversations; a sports board game with written rules; spelling grid: once, twice, sometimes, often; and the "I CAN — Name sports and other activities" page with a do/does reminder (*Use does in questions using he or she*) and sport-definition reading.

**Unit 4 (pp. 52–55).** Revision: completing country names and a countries snakes-and-ladders board; "Check your progress" sentence completion from a word box; labelling the camel (hump, ear, tail, feet, eyelash, nose); matching animals to adjectives; "Read Dima's diary. Correct seven mistakes" — a diary planted with wrong collocations and prepositions (*I always go my homework, I often play shopping, I see TV, a picnic in the river, go to bed on 10 o'clock*); and a 14-picture listen-and-order activity set.

**Unit 5 (pp. 56–71).** Sorting food words by syllables (1/2/3) and a food crossword; reading a "Class D favourite food" pie chart; naming containers (packet, cup, box, tin, bottle, bag); marking phrases countable or uncountable with a LOOK box contrasting the substances (water, tea, lemonade, rice, eggs, biscuits) with their container phrases (*bottle of water, cup of tea, tin of lemonade, bag of rice, box of eggs, packet of biscuits*); a shop scene to read and role-play (*What would you like? — Can I have a box of eggs, please? I'd like four bananas, please*); unscrambling some/any sentences with their punctuation; food word diagrams; ordering an *I'd prefer* picnic conversation; a health survey scored healthy/unhealthy and two Activity-Book-only routine texts (Ridha and Marwa) to score and write from; completing vegetable names from their vowels; a full **plural-rules LOOK table** (add -s; add -es after ch, sh, s, x, z; consonant + y → -ies, *country → countries*; most -o words add -s, *kangaroo → kangaroos*, but some add -es, *tomato → tomatoes*); spelling conversations; picnic vocabulary phrases; a LOOK box contrasting present and past (*I catch a fish. Dad catches a fish. Yesterday, I caught a fish*); verb-ending exercises mixing present -s and past forms; a choose-your-own picnic story built from alternative phrases; a past-tense reading text, "Saturday by Dima", with the food sorted into fridge/kitchen/supermarket pictures, partner questions (*Where were the tomatoes?*), circling all the past verbs and writing past forms (go, look, need, want, make, cook); spelling grid: some, half, would, any; and the "I CAN" page with a "Remember: How many …? for countable / How much …? for uncountable" box, there-is/are completion and container lists.

**Unit 6 (pp. 72–87).** Building shop names from syllable chunks; a to-do list matched to shops; listening to spatial relations and writing sentences about blocks (in front of, behind, next to, opposite); an 'ee' vs 'e' sound sort (bakery, green, street, team vs egg, next, red, help); labelling a street map from preposition sentences; word-order sentences and *Excuse me* direction dialogues; "Time or place?" — a LOOK box giving in/on/at for both time and place (in April/October, on Monday/Saturday, at 4 o'clock/the weekend; in Baghdad/London, on Red Street/Green Street, at 14 Al-Rashid Street); an Activity-Book-only past-tense text, Sardar's trip to the city by train, with the past verbs underlined and then rewritten in the present simple; a Fun City crossword; making *Would you like* questions from a diagram; matching famous sights to countries (Big Ben, the pyramids, Malwiya, the Statue of Liberty, Sydney Opera House); souvenir anagrams and price work (*How much is the poster? — It's 4000 IQD*; computing what Jena, Dima, Karam and Firas bought and spent); a LOOK box of ordinals (4th–10th regular, but 1st, 2nd, 3rd); a second LOOK box introducing **did-questions** (*What did you do yesterday? — I visited Water World. Did you visit Water World? — Yes, I did. / No, I didn't*); ordering a Babylon postcard; checking facts against the Pupil's Book adverts; spelling grid: first, third, behind, opposite; and the "I CAN" page (shop names, preposition diagrams, a "Remember" list of past forms: be → was/were, buy, do, drive, eat, go, visit) with word sorting into adjectives / fun rides / shops and stalls.

**Unit 7 (pp. 88–103).** Season words from missing letters; reading a weather map of an island (mountains, marshes, desert) and placing four climate texts; completing *going to* sentences (including *visit the Citadel in Erbil* and *learn some Kurdish words*); choosing question words for going-to questions (who/when/how/where/why/what); a tidy-up dialogue to number, built on *This CD isn't ours, Dad. It's yours!* and *That's my bag. It's mine, not yours*; a LOOK box "Talking about whose things" (*Whose bag is this? — It's my bag. OR It's mine.* — with the noun after *mine* shown crossed out); converting Pupil's Book p. 95 sentences between the adjective and pronoun forms; present-giving with *it/them* (*I'm going to give them to my Dad*) and guessing presents from replies; reading Jena's Erbil holiday notebook (snow, a snowman, presents for grandfather/grandmother/friends, postcards of the Bekhal waterfall) and writing your own notebook from a frame; a memory quiz on the Pupil's Book magazine texts; a LOOK box contrasting regular verbs ("end in -ed — Yesterday, I walked to school") with irregular verbs ("have no pattern — I swam in the sea") and writing past forms marked R or I (is/are, tell, take, make, travel, visit, love, fly, look); word-and-punctuation ordering of travel sentences; a cloze on the magazine texts; mistake sentences with exactly one error each (*I swam on the sea. I went on plane. We eated our picnic food. … because it was wind*); spelling grid: high, through, think, thought; and the "I CAN" page marking a *Karam's dream* homework text with the correction symbols, "My new words", and choosing the correct verb forms (present, past and going to contrasted).

**Unit 8 (pp. 104–110) and end matter.** Revision: circling the odd word out and naming the word groups (countries, irregular verbs, sports, adjectives, about our world, land animals); a "Let's remember" countable/uncountable check (*You can't count lemonade, but you can count tins of lemonade*); a birthday-party invitation email to read and answer in writing (would-like choices, directions to the sports club, going-to plans); the closing board game "I can say more in English!" (25 prompts across the whole course); blank Spelling tests grids for Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 (p. 108); and Game circle 1 (p. 109) and Game circle 2 (p. 110) — this volume prints only two game circles, where the Grade 4 book prints four. The back cover (PDF p. 112) describes the five course components.

### Grammar spine

The workbook states the rules the Pupil's Book practises: the *be* forms and nationality endings (with irregular Thai/French); do/does question tables; the three sounds of verb -s; and/or after negatives; frequency phrases; countable vs uncountable with container phrases; the full plural-spelling table; present -s against past forms (catch/caught); in/on/at for time and place; ordinals; and — beyond the Pupil's Book's modelling — explicit boxes for **did-questions** (*What did you do yesterday? Did you visit …? — Yes, I did / No, I didn't*) and for **regular vs irregular past verbs**, plus the possessive-pronoun rule that *mine/yours/his/hers* stand alone. Its skills spine is equally systematic: alphabetical order, syllable counts, -teen/-ty and -s-ending pronunciation, vowel-gap spelling, per-unit spelling grids, and end-of-unit "I CAN" self-checks with teacher-style correction symbols.

## Grade 5 — English for Iraq 5th Primary Teacher's Book (teacher's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH in substance, with the series' own naming. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Teacher's Guide"; the book itself is titled **Teacher's Book** on its cover.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 5th Primary — Teacher's Book* (front cover).
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie (cover); the imprint asserts MacBurnie's authorial rights alone. The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha, Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid, Faten Fakhri Khalaf, Hanaa Adil Ali.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., Reading, UK (imprint); cover: Garnet Education; issued for the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition © 2017" (imprint, PDF p. 3). Production code: IRQ17 TB 5thP. The back cover is dated 2017 and lists the components (Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Teacher's Book, Audio, Posters).
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed. The back cover carries a publisher's barcode with the product code TRG003866 — a stock code, not an ISBN.
- **PDF pages:** 194, all accounted for (see method note in Honest negatives).
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** This file opens with a **third-party wrapper cover** (PDF p. 1): a skewed duplicate of the cover carrying "Education for Iraq" (E4I) roundel logos and partly different committee details — it is not part of the printed book. PDF p. 2 is the book's own cover, PDF p. 3 the imprint, and from there **printed page = PDF page − 1**: the Introduction is printed p. 5 (PDF p. 6), Teaching Notes Unit 1 begins at printed p. 33 (PDF p. 34), the appendices run to the Game Circles at printed p. 190, and PDF p. 194 is the back cover. The printed book is 193 pages plus covers. All page references below are to the printed pages.

- **Third-party marks in this scan copy:** the E4I ("Education for Iraq") roundel logo is stamped on many pages of this copy (over the wrapper cover, at the foot of the Introduction, across unit front pages, on the Spelling Grids page), an "E4I" footer tag appears on numerous pages, "Education4IQ" watermark text sits at the foot of the imprint page, and Arabic "Education for Iraq website" text is stamped over the back-cover barcode and beside the Garnet logo. These are marks of the distributing website's scan, not of the printed book, and they occasionally sit on top of the book's own header art.
- **Design:** Bilingual (English pages mirrored by Arabic pages), same architecture as the Grade 4 and Grade 6 teacher's books. Components for Grade 5: Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Your Dictionary (English–Arabic), Teacher's Book, Teacher Resource File audio and A2 posters — the Grade 4 mini Picture/Word Cards are no longer listed, and the Key features replace Grade 4's sound–letter revision line with "Pronunciation practice". The spelling system continues: four high-frequency words per unit with "Look, say, cover, write, check" grids and a spelling test opening the next unit's Lesson 1. New in the Grade 5 aims is explicit attention to self-correction: Units 2 and 7 both list "Look at correction marks and think about correcting your own writing" (the teacher's correction symbols V/SP/C/WO/WW that the Activity Book teaches).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Introduction (pp. 5–31).** The series front matter (Key features, Components, The course, Aims, Advice to teachers, Teaching the course) at the Year 5 level: the course builds "on the foundation that has been laid in the first four years", stories remain a topic-linked feature of every unit, and the Teacher Resource File carries the key vocabulary recordings so teachers can practise pronunciation. The **Book Map (pp. 30–31)** prints each unit's aims and key language: Unit 1 (countries/nationalities, *What nationality are you?*, *I want to learn English because …*); Unit 2 (telling the time, "Focus on the –s in 3rd person (present simple tense)", *What's a shark like? It's dangerous*, HFWs two, eight, does, goes); Unit 3 (play/go/do, frequency, *once a week, twice a month*, going to for TV programmes); Unit 4 (Revision of Units 1–3); Unit 5 (is there/are there with countable and uncountable food, containers, *Would you like some salad? … I'd prefer tea*, plural spelling rules, HFWs some, half, would, any); Unit 6 (shops and prepositions of place, *Where was Rahaf at 10.45?*, would-like invitations, ordinals 1–10, HFWs first, third, behind, opposite); Unit 7 (seasons and weather, going to plans, possessive pronouns, *It's her pencil case. / It's hers.*, HFWs high, through, think, thought); Unit 8 (Revision of Units 5–7).

**Unit 1 Teaching Notes (pp. 33–69).** Aims by skill and the Language and Word list (countries Thailand, France, Japan, Egypt, England, Iraq, Turkey, Italy; nationalities Egyptian, Italian, Iraqi, English, Turkish, Japanese, Thai, French; talking-about-countries words including kleicha, macaroni, pandas, elephants, curry, masgouf, kangaroo; adjectives famous, high, interesting, beautiful). The lesson notes follow the fixed pattern (Aims/Materials box, Warm up, Stages across PB and AB, transcripts and answers in the notes, Extend/Support boxes); Lesson 1 introduces the new cast through Dima ("Hello, my name's Dima. I'm from Iraq") and the country-names track.

**Unit 2 Teaching Notes (pp. 70–89).** Times and routines. The aims make the year's most explicit grammar demand — focus on the third-person -s — and add the writing aims "Review alphabetical order" and the correction-marks aim; the magazine feature is *Insects in our life*, and the notes cover the dolphin essay and the Dove project from the Pupil's Book.

**Unit 3 Teaching Notes (pp. 90–109).** Sports: verb + sport collocations (play/go/do), frequency language and survey reading, the *Sports are great* magazine, the TV programme guide and the sports project, with the story-retelling aim ("Retell a story in your own words") that recurs across this book.

**Unit 4 Teaching Notes (pp. 110–117).** Revision of Units 1–3 built around the camel: the aims are all "Review:" items, and the unit's only word list is "Talking about a camel" (Arabian, Asian, hump, soft, fat, stay cool, hairy, thick, eyelashes, protect, calf, born) — the notes service the challenge, Firas's email correction and the diary correction in the Pupil's and Activity Books.

**Unit 5 Teaching Notes (pp. 118–137).** Food and drink: countable/uncountable identification, containers, offers and preferences, the health survey and the *Picnic by the lake* magazine and story; writing aims include "Look at how you spell plural nouns", tying the notes to the Activity Book's plural-rules table.

**Unit 6 Teaching Notes (pp. 138–159).** Shops, directions and the past: prepositions of place (behind, next to, opposite, between), in/on/at for time and place, sentence stress as a named listening aim, was/were with clock times, would-like invitations, the Baghdad and Babel postcards and the *A fun day out* magazine feature.

**Unit 7 Teaching Notes (pp. 160–177).** Seasons, holidays and presents: going to for plans, possessive adjectives and pronouns, Jena's Erbil notebook and the *How we travel* magazine, holiday recounts in the past, and the correction-marks writing aim again — the notes support the Activity Book's task of correcting *Karam's dream* with the V/SP/C/WO/WW symbols.

**Unit 8 Teaching Notes (pp. 178–185).** Revision of Units 5–7: the class challenge, the 'I can say more in English' game, Rayan's email and the Goodbye Party song. The unit front page's heading is misprinted **"Language and World list"** (for "Language and Word list"), and its content is given in full on the front page: "Language: Revision of Units 5–7. Revision of 5th Primary. Word list: Review of vocabulary from 5th Primary."

**Appendices (pp. 186–193).** The **Teacher Resource File Transcript** (pp. 186–188) prints Tracks 111–117: the key language of Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 and Track 117, "Useful classroom language". Then the photocopiable **Spelling Grids** (p. 189, matching the Activity Book) and **Game Circles** (from p. 190), closing the book.

### Grammar spine

As in Grade 4, the Teacher's Book presents the pupil grammar as functional chunks and keeps its own contribution at the level of aims, transcripts, answers and procedure. What it adds for the record is the year's explicitness gradient: Grade 5 is where the course first names a tense target in its aims ("Focus on the –s in 3rd person (present simple tense)"), first teaches plural spelling rules and correction symbols as writing aims, and first asks pupils to retell stories in their own words. The pupil-facing sequence is the Pupil's Book's (see the Grade 5 Pupil's Book filing): present simple as a system, countable/uncountable with some/any, would like/prefer, was/were, going to, possessive pronouns.

## Grade 6 — English for Iraq 6th Primary Pupil's Book (pupil's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH in substance, with the series' own naming. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Student's Book"; the book itself is titled **Pupil's Book** on its cover.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 6th Primary — Pupil's Book* (front cover).
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie (cover and imprint). The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha (Senior Education Specialist), Hanaa Adil Ali (Teacher of English), Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid (Senior Education Specialist), Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Supervisor). The imprint thanks the translator Khulood Aswad Shmes.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (imprint). Issued for the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula; the cover carries the Arabic free-distribution notice.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition © 2018 Garnet Publishing Ltd." (imprint). Production code: IRQ18 PB 6thP. The back cover is dated 2018 and lists the course components (Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Teacher's Book, Audio, Posters).
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed. The back cover carries a publisher's barcode with the product code IRQ004223 — a stock code, not an ISBN.
- **PDF pages:** 122, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** This file binds **two cover scans** (PDF p. 1 blue-background, PDF p. 2 white-background), so from the imprint (PDF p. 3) onward **printed page = PDF page − 1**: Unit 1 Lesson 1 is printed p. 6 at PDF p. 7, the "Your Dictionary" runs printed pp. 110–120, and PDF p. 122 is the back cover. The printed book is 120 pages. All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text-layer character:** None — image-only scan (pdftotext returns only form feeds). Read whole from 100-DPI 4-up contact sheets; a RapidOCR pass over the renders serves as a search index. Clean colour scan, no scanner wrapper.
- **Cast/design:** Full-colour course book, 8 units of 8 lessons (Units 4 and 8 are 4-lesson revision units). The Grade 6 cast is a Baghdad family: Wisam (12), his sister Dania (13), little brother Ammar (6), twin cousins Basim and Hiba, their parents (father an IT programmer, mother a nurse and housewife) and grandparents. The lesson architecture matches Grades 4–5 (Book Map pp. 3–5, "My Magazine", "Check my understanding", projects, a Lesson 8 story or song) but the reading texts are longer and the grammar is handled comparatively — tenses and forms are set against each other rather than introduced one at a time.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 (pp. 6–21) — Family and jobs.** A family tree with the full relationship set (mother/mum/mummy, father/dad/daddy, grandfather/grandpa, grandmother/grandma/granny, brother, sister, cousin, uncle, aunt/auntie, twin), Wisam's family text (*My sister's name is Dania. She is older than me — she's 13*), and a "Let's check" table for *How old is …?* against *How many brothers/sisters/cousins does … have?*. Lessons 2–3 teach ten jobs (engineer, IT programmer, farmer, teacher, housewife, shop assistant, nurse, bus driver, waiter, doctor, plus builder) with a/an, *What's your dad's job? — He's an engineer*, and workplaces with a preposition table: work **in** a hospital/office/restaurant/oil refinery, **on** the roads/a building site/a farm, **at** home. Lesson 4 is the reading text "My family routine" (the parents' working week, question formation — "Remember to use question marks"). Lesson 5, "My favourite job", teaches *When I grow up, I want to be a(n) … because …*. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Two families" — Han, a Chinese farming boy, and Farah, whose father is an Iraqi Airlines pilot — checked in Lesson 7 with pronoun completion (she/he/they/we/it). Lesson 8 is the story *When I grow up*, in which everyone predicts the narrator's future job in *will* sentences.

**Unit 2 (pp. 22–37) — Materials and shopping.** *It's made of …* — materials (glass, wood, leather, metal, rubber, wool, cotton, denim) and the material adjectives (wooden, woollen), with a singular/plural "Remember" (*What's the jug made of? — It's made of glass. What are the jeans made of? — They're made of denim*), and clothing labelling. Lesson 2, "At the mall", locates departments by floor (men's/women's/children's fashion, home cooking, IT and computing, café, supermarket, play area; ground/first/second floor). Lesson 3, "Can I help you?", is shop dialogue built on this/that/these/those, sizes and prices (*It's too big. It's too expensive. About 900,000 IQD*). Lesson 4, "Opposites", pairs adjectives (hard/soft, plain/colourful, cheap/expensive, thick/thin, light/thick, long/short, big/small) and joins descriptions with *and/but*. Lesson 5, "Materials", is a Fun Facts reading (wood, rubber, cotton — "The word cotton comes from the Arabic word kutun" — leather, cashmere wool from Iraq among other countries) with a "Remember" box distinguishing *be*-questions (Is …?/Are …?) from questions with the "helping verb" Do/Does, and turning statements into questions. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: A blog — Working in a busy hospital", a heart surgeon's blog that deliberately contrasts the two presents: *What does she do most days? — She works in the hospital* vs *What are you doing now? — I'm spending time with my family*; Lesson 7 checks it by circling the correct tense (*I work / am working with a small team*). The project is a school-backpack poster ad ("Our school backpack"), and Lesson 8 is the song "Think about your materials carefully".

**Unit 3 (pp. 38–53) — Health and the internet.** Body parts and ailments: *How are you today? — I don't feel well. I feel hot and I have a headache. What's the matter?* with the ailment set (a broken arm, a cough, a toothache, a sore throat, a temperature, a stomach ache, feel sick, a cold, a rash). Lesson 2, "You should …", gives advice (*You should go to bed. You should take some medicine. You shouldn't eat so much cake*) inside a mother-and-son dialogue that also uses *will* naturally (*Will I feel better soon? — Yes, I'm sure you'll feel better in the morning*). Lesson 3, "Helping friends", completes should/shouldn't advice for pictured problems — including the reflexives in the stimulus sentences (*She cut her finger. My little brother burnt himself*) — and reads Ahmad's past-tense email about his bad day, then matches problems to advice with *because/so*. Lesson 4, "Using the internet", teaches the web vocabulary as a verbs/nouns table (access, go online, search, key in, press, come up, navigate, scroll up/down, click on; the internet, login, keyboard, webpage, screen, mouse, touchscreen, an address, the web). Lesson 5, "Going online", has Hiba teach her grandparents to search (coughsandcolds, homecakes) in two completion conversations. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Stay safe online" — the "6 ways to stay safe online" (be nice, be careful what you share, keep personal information and passwords safe, never meet online-only contacts, tell an adult) plus a page on learning English with online games. Lesson 7 checks understanding and drills the difference between *must* and *should* by circling. The project is a "Healthy habits for healthy children" poster, and Lesson 8 is the story *Looking after Ammar* — present-continuous narration of the knife accident (*The knife slips and cuts his finger … You mustn't use sharp knives!*).

**Unit 4 (pp. 54–57) — Revision of Units 1–3.** The class challenge; "My progress" with the factual text *A computer mouse* (why it is called a mouse, buttons and wheel, how to hold it — instructions in *You should* bullets); "My homework" — Wisam's five-paragraph letter about himself and his family as a ten-gap cloze; and a board game.

**Unit 5 (pp. 58–73) — Change, inventions and space.** Lesson 1, "Past, present and future", organises the whole tense story in three panels: *When I was younger … my mum dressed me / Now … I dress myself. I can feed myself / In the future … I will buy my own clothes. I will be an IT programmer*, introducing the reflexive *myself* and *my own* alongside *will*. Lesson 2, "The past", reads about inventions (the telephone — Bell 1876; the car — Benz 1885–1886; computers — Al-Bairuni in the 11th century, Babbage, Lovelace). Lesson 3, "Living now", matches pupils' opinions about change (*Computers are getting more intelligent. Everything is getting faster*, with *getting* + comparative). Lesson 4, "Our 21st century", predicts with *will/won't* (robots, robot teachers, driverless cars, smartphones for money) and frames opinions (*I think … will … / I don't think this will happen*). Lesson 5, "Space", reads *Our solar system* and sets the comparative/superlative system out in a "Let's check" box, including the irregular sets far/farther/farthest and close/closer/closest, then answers questions from pictures. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Space travel", an interview about Mars (*Humans have landed on the Moon six times … In the future, I think humans will go to Mars*). Lesson 7 checks it and completes *I think … will/won't …* predictions about 2050. Lesson 8 is the rhyme "Our solar system" and a Space board game.

**Unit 6 (pp. 74–89) — Cooking and safety.** Cooking verbs (add, spread, grate, peel, bake, mix, wash, slice) and the participle adjectives (sliced onions, grated cheese, peeled orange). Lesson 2, "Make it yourself", is a full recipe — *Easy flatbread pizza* — ingredients list and imperative instructions to complete with the cooking verbs, prefaced by a safety warning (*you must ask your parents before you cook anything or use knives*). Lesson 3, "Cooking at home", completes a family conversation (can/mustn't/going to: *Mum, can we make a pizza? … you mustn't use a knife. I'm going to grate the cheese*). Lesson 4 covers road safety (the zebra-crossing rhyme, sorting nine poster photos into good/bad behaviour, seatbelts and road signals) and home safety ("Let's be safe at home": the *Accidents at home* text — falls, burns, knives and glass, medicines — and writing the safety rules with *Keep … away from children*). Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Stay safe on your bike" (brakes, handlebars, seat, tyres, pedals, helmet; nine safe-riding rules in must/should). Lesson 7 checks it, the project is the home-safety poster, and Lesson 8, "Our rules", completes the children's own safety rules and plays The safety game.

**Unit 7 (pp. 90–105) — Celebrations.** A Mother's Day party drives the unit: Lesson 1, "Getting ready for our party", works from a to-do list (do the shopping, clean the house, do the cooking, buy presents, wrap presents, put up decorations, make a card) and Dania's email full of *going to* plans. Lesson 2, "Shopping for our party", completes the shopping-list conversation (presents for Granny Nadia, Granny Huda and Mum — a leather bag, a wooden picture frame) and labels a mall from written clues. Lesson 3, "Presents for Mother's Day", labels gifts with the material adjectives (woollen scarf, wooden frame, metal watch, leather bag) and chooses wrapping paper by listening. Lesson 4, "Which job?", allocates the party jobs and completes a family conversation of *should/must* instructions (*Wisam, you should blow up the balloons. But first you must tidy up your bedroom*). Lesson 5, "Saturday morning", is a present-continuous question set (*Mum is in the kitchen. What's she doing? — She's baking a cake*) with listening inference about party talk. Lesson 6 is "My Magazine: Making a card" (why make your own card; the card-giving calendar — Mother's Day 21st March, Teacher's Day 1st March, birthdays, New Year 1st January, congratulations — and instructions to order). Lesson 7 checks it with verb-collocation matching, the project is the Mother's Day card poster, and Lesson 8 is the story *A card for our teacher*, narrated in the past tense.

**Unit 8 (pp. 106–109) — Revision of Units 5–7.** The class challenge (past habits, getting-comparatives, will/won't, cooking verbs, safety rules, present continuous); "My progress" with the reading text *Teacher's Day* (1st March in Iraq, thank-you notes and cards); "An email to my grandparents" — Hiba's end-of-Year-6 email as a cloze that recycles the whole book; and the closing song "When I grow up".

**Your Dictionary (pp. 110–120).** An English–Arabic wordlist by unit for all eight units; Unit Four contributes only three words (bat, net (not a fishing net), table tennis) and Unit Eight four (Intermediate school, recipe, seed, some day).

### Grammar spine

Grade 6 consolidates the primary course and adds its final contrasts. New or newly systematised: the present simple against the present continuous, taught as a choice (the surgeon's blog: *What does she do most days?* vs *What are you doing now?*; party preparations in Unit 7); *will/won't* for predictions with *I think …* (Units 1, 5), alongside *going to* for plans (Unit 7); *getting* + comparative (Unit 5); the full comparative/superlative system with the far/close irregular sets (Unit 5); *should/shouldn't* for advice against *must/mustn't* for rules (Units 3, 6, 7); reflexives in use (*I dress myself, She cut herself, young children can cut themselves*) and *my own* (Unit 5); *made of* vs *made from* in the materials texts (Units 2, 5); question formation split by verb type (*be* inverts; other verbs take Do/Does) (Unit 2); a/an with jobs and in/on/at with workplaces (Unit 1); dates (*It's on 7th April. It's on Monday*) recycled from the course's earlier books. The past simple carries all the unit stories and recounts.

### Citation-check section

### Honest negatives

- **No passive voice and no conditionals** are taught anywhere in the book (the nearest approach is the road-crossing rhyme's "If the road is safe, cross with a friend", which is not presented as grammar).
- **The present perfect is never taught, but it is used**: the space texts say *Humans have landed on the Moon six times and twelve astronauts have walked on it* and *There have been inventions to help calculation for thousands of years*, and Lesson 7's check questions repeat *Humans have walked on the Moon* — a pupil meets the form only inside reading matter, with no paradigm or explanation anywhere in the primary course.
- **No reported speech** (the *When I grow up* story frames predictions as "my teacher says I will be an engineer" without any reporting grammar), **no question tags, no future continuous or perfect forms**.
- **No phonics or spelling strand in the Pupil's Book** (carried by the Activity Book); dates and times are recycled, not extended.
- Copy/scan notes: this copy is complete (122/122 PDF pages) but binds two cover scans, shifting printed folios one behind the PDF pages — handled in the offset above. No ISBN is printed; the back-cover barcode is a product code (IRQ004223).
