## Grade 6 — English for Iraq 6th Primary Activity Book (activity book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH in substance. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Activity Book"; the imprint production code (IRQ18 **AB** 6thP) and the workbook design confirm the Grade 6 Activity Book. Note: in this greyscale scan the cover's "Activity Book" label box (bottom right) is washed out to a blank textured panel, so the kind rests on the imprint code and contents rather than the cover wording.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 6th Primary* (front cover, PDF p. 1); the volume is the Activity Book (see verdict note).
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie (cover and imprint). The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha (Senior Education Specialist), Hanaa Adil Ali (Teacher of English), Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid (Senior Education Specialist), Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Supervisor). The imprint thanks the translator Khulood Aswad Shmes.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (imprint); cover: Garnet Education, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula, with the Arabic free-distribution notice.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition © 2018 Garnet Publishing Ltd." (imprint, PDF p. 2). Production code: IRQ18 AB 6thP. The back cover lists the course components (Pupil's Book, Activity Book, Teacher's Book, Audio, Posters).
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed. The back cover carries a publisher's barcode with the product code TRG004224 — a stock code, not an ISBN.
- **PDF pages:** 113, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** Printed page = PDF page (offset 0): cover PDF p. 1, imprint PDF p. 2, "This book belongs to" PDF p. 3, Unit 1 Lesson 1 on printed p. 4 (PDF p. 4), folios running to Game circle 4 on printed p. 112; PDF p. 113 is the back cover. All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text-layer character:** None — image-only greyscale scan. Read whole from 100-DPI 4-up contact sheets, with a RapidOCR pass over the renders as a search index. No scanner wrapper. A handful of light-grey activity panels (the Unit 1 "I CAN" sentence boxes, some word tiles) print very faintly in this scan.
- **Cast/design:** Black-and-white workbook with lettered exercises (A, B, C …) for every Pupil's Book lesson, LOOK boxes stating rules, "Let's say it" pronunciation pages, per-unit spelling grids, and "I CAN …" / "MY NEW WORDS" closers. Its level is visibly higher than the Grade 5 workbook: it teaches adjective order, homophones, abbreviations, dictionary-style word entries, reported opinions and how to write an email, and it carries substantial reading texts of its own.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Unit 1 (pp. 4–19).** Matching male/female family words and building a family tree; a LOOK box of informal family names (*mother OR mum/mummy, father OR dad/daddy, grandmother OR grandma/granny, grandfather OR grandpa, aunt OR auntie*); writing about your own family from Wisam's model; completing job names from their endings (-er, -or, -ist: doct-or, wait-er, teach-er, engin-eer, programm-er, nurs-e …); writing *Is he a waiter? — No, he isn't a waiter. He's a doctor* sentences; finding one mistake in each family sentence (*I have two cousin. I has an aunt. My brother is old than me. My cousin has 13*); a pronunciation page on -er/-or words ("make the same sound") with stress marking; "What's my job?" — four job-description riddles (doctor, builder, farmer, waiter) to identify and imitate; an Activity-Book-only text, "A waiter's day" (Ahmed in a Baghdad restaurant famous for makhlama, quzi and biriyani); a long present-simple text where pupils choose every verb form (is/are, work/works, leave/leaves, take/takes, has/have …) and order a family's day by its times; the jobs survey chart judged true/false with superlative opinions (*the most interesting, the easiest*); completing a jobs conversation; correcting spelling mistakes (docter, houswife, nurs, bus diver); an Activity-Book-only text about Hiba and her family with write-about-yourself; a **Punctuation box** (capital letters: start of sentences, after a full stop, names of people, places and nationalities, "also for other names, for example: airlines, shops, restaurants, hospitals"); an Activity-Book-only text about a teacher's job with an information file to complete; spelling grid: aunt, uncle, easy, interesting; and the "I CAN / MY NEW WORDS" pages (job wordsearch, job riddles).

**Unit 2 (pp. 20–35).** Listening for what things are made of and writing descriptions (*They are colourful cotton towels*); a LOOK box that is unique to this book — **the order of adjectives: size → colour → material** (*a(n) big black leather sofa*) — then colouring and describing a room in writing; ordering two mall conversations and answering questions on them; reading Class A's mall survey; writing shop advertisements from a word bank (*We're NOT expensive! Our prices are great!*); matching this/that/these/those comparative comments to a clothes-shop picture (*This is pretty. That is prettier. These are too short. Those are bigger*); choosing adjectives and writing descriptive phrases (*a soft ice-cream*); describing your own desk and chair; a LOOK homophone page (to/two, with too introduced in the follow-up circling work); matching *comes from* against *is made/are made from* (*A plant comes from a seed. A pot is made from clay*); writing material sentences and upgrading them with *most, some, always, often, usually*; counting *and* and *but* in the Pupil's Book blog, with Venn-style diagrams and a LOOK box (*We use but to show a contrast … We use and to add information*); an Activity-Book-only reading text, "The history of paper" (China, Egyptian papyrus, what paper is made from, recycling and conserving trees); true/false on it; spelling grid: only, enough, many, usually; and a "MY NEW WORDS" page circling to/too/two, and/but and this/that/these/those in context, closing with a materials crossword.

**Unit 3 (pp. 36–51).** Labelling the body, with a pronunciation note on silent letters (knee — silent 'k'; thumb — silent 'b') and irregular plurals (tooth/teeth, foot/feet); writing *He has … / She has …* ailment sentences; matching *You should …* advice; completing ailment words and identifying the sounds of ch/th/gh/sh inside them (ch as /k/ in stomach ache, gh as /f/ in cough); an Activity-Book-only text, "What do you do when you have a cough and a cold? Here are some ideas from around the world" (ginger in China, onion in Japan, honey drinks, garlic in Spain, a hot egg-and-honey drink in Russia); building verb phrases (put a plaster on, break your arm, burn yourself, feel sick …) and completing advice sentences; the course's fullest grammar box — **reflexive pronouns**: "When the subject and the object in a sentence are the same, we use a pronoun+self (or pronoun+selves). I see my+self in the mirror. You can't say: I see me. You say: I see myself", with the whole paradigm (myself, yourself, himself/herself/itself, ourselves, yourselves, themselves), completion exercises and "The selfie rhyme"; matching internet icons to their meanings and verbs to noun phrases; a **guide to writing an email** (address, subject, "Hi + name", first paragraph, new paragraph for something new, "Best wishes") with a model and space to write your own; finding the one wrong web word in each sentence (interworld, onweb, webplace, key out, clock on, dress for address, school up, house for mouse) with a LOOK box on *the* (*the internet, the web* — "because there is only one" — but *a website, an address*; also the Sun, the Moon, the world, the Euphrates, the Tigris); a cloze text "Colds and coughs can make you feel very unwell" and the "Sneezing rhyme"; a LOOK box defining the modal contrast (**should/shouldn't — "to say what is the best thing to do (give advice)"; must/mustn't — "to make a rule"**); an Activity-Book-only text, "My family's new laptop by Basim"; story-sentence ordering for *Looking after Ammar*; spelling grid: knee, thumb, stomach, cough.

**Unit 4 (pp. 52–55).** Revision: completing question sentences across Units 1–3; a bus driver's day as a picture-sequencing text; labelling a table-tennis table by materials (rubber wheels, metal legs, a cotton net, a plastic ball, a wooden table top, a wooden bat); "Read a boy's story" — a past-tense story with eight wrong words to replace (mall → school, bigger → better …); and ordering "My grandpa's story about going online".

**Unit 5 (pp. 56–71).** Sorting invention photos into past/present/future (telephones, bicycles, planes); an Activity-Book-only text, "A fun invention that everyone likes" — George Crum inventing potato chips in 1853 — with multiple-choice questions; making a string telephone from instructions; a pronunciation/word-building page on **-tion nouns** (invention, communication, conversation, calculation, information, navigation, description, instruction — "What do all these words end in?") with spelling corrections against the Pupil's Book (posible, telephon, inventer, peeple, centuri, inportant); a dictionary-style table (Word / Type of word / Meaning: population, price(s), height, number) with diagrams to match and change sentences to complete (*Prices are going up. People are getting taller*); go up/increase/get + comparative against go down/decrease; "Read Basim's homework" — the family's future predictions written as reported opinions (*They think that computers will be more intelligent. He says that driverless cars will be safer too. She doesn't think that there will be robot teachers*); writing will/won't sentences from it; the planets in order with the mnemonic "My Very Excellent Macaroni Jelly Stands Up Now" and true/false space facts; comparatives pictured (tall/taller/the tallest, nice/nicer/the nicest, heavy/heavier/the heaviest) with the **comparative spelling table** (short adjectives + er/est; ending in -e + r/st; -y → -ier/-iest); a dates "Remember" box (in + month, on + day, at + time/holiday — in April, on Saturday, at 4 o'clock, at Eid) with years and centuries to sort; ordering will-sentences; writing comparatives and superlatives; homophone sets (son/sun, father/farther, it's/its, you're/your, they're/there/their, plus to/too/two, one/won, here/hear, by/buy) used in sentences; spelling grid: easy, easier, heavy, heavier; and a space crossword ("What's the mystery planet?").

**Unit 6 (pp. 72–87).** Matching cooking verbs to tools; choosing the right verb for cooking sentences; unjumbling the spellings of cooking verbs hidden in anagrams (kebsa → bakes, dixme → mixed, lisec → slice, sawh → wash, elep → peel, edspar → spread); odd-one-out with reasons; a LOOK box on **abbreviations** (g — gram, kg — kilogram, sp — spoon; mm, cm, m; km) with *how far / how tall / how wide*; an Activity-Book-only recipe, "Easy planet biscuits" (100g butter, 280g flour …), its instructions to order; a housework to-do list matched to a messy house; completing a recipe-hunt conversation; matching mixed-up safety rules and a LOOK box on **instructions** ("The verb usually comes at the beginning of the sentence, like Stop and Look. We can also add Do not/Don't"); the Instruction game; naming home dangers (gas bottle, oven gloves, broken glass, cleaning materials, hot saucepans, clothes iron, matches); completing Mum's *Don't …* kitchen rules; a LOOK box on giving reasons (**Situation / Reason: because + verb + noun** — "The reason tells us why"); a second comparative spelling table (doubling the consonant: thin → thinner, fat → fatter; long adjectives with more/most; the irregular rows good, bad, far left to complete); completing the parents' handwritten safety notes; writing your own notes; road-safety bookmarks as the "I CAN" page; spelling grid: should, shouldn't, must, mustn't; and "My special pizza" — writing your own recipe card.

**Unit 7 (pp. 88–103).** Completing the party to-do phrases and writing present-continuous sentences from pictures (*She's …*); correcting the twelve mistakes in Hiba's party email (at/on Saturday, won't → will be a surprise, sitting house → room, tidy her → his bedroom, make → buy the presents, Dad but Wisam → and, put down → up the decorations, bake → make a card, grandpa's shopping → presents, two → too busy, tell you for → about the party); a choose-your-own shopping story; true/false on a town map; two Activity-Book-only reading texts (buying presents for a cousin in Basra — brown leather boots and a green and blue cotton T-shirt; a little sister's get-well presents — a soft woollen teddy bear), mined for a Presents/Colour/Material/Wrapping paper table that practises the adjective order; writing about a present you are going to buy; a verb table giving past forms, regular/irregular judgements and should/must sentences (blow up/blew up I; tidy up/tidied up R; take/took; buy/bought …); correcting broken present-continuous sentences (*He get on the bus. She's push his daughter. He will put on a helmet*); a celebrations crossword; an Activity-Book-only "Mother's Day" text (the date differs around the world; in Iraq it comes in March); matching card messages to their occasions (Happy New Year, Happy Teacher's Day, Happy Birthday, Congratulations, Congratulations on passing your exams); listening about Dania's card and planning your own; making an envelope from measured instructions; spelling grid: woollen, wooden, leather, metal; and the "I CAN" page ordering the *A card for our teacher* story dialogue.

**Unit 8 (pp. 104–112) and end matter.** Revision: odd-word-out and naming the word groups (family, adjectives, materials, IT and the internet, technology, feeling unwell); writing picture descriptions from word boxes; a spelling game to read and play (*It begins with b, it ends with e. You do it in an oven. — Is it bake?*); the closing board game "I can say much more in English!" (20 prompts, ending "You've finished Grade 6. We say: Well done and congratulations!"); blank Spelling tests grids for Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 (p. 108); and Game circles 1–4 (pp. 109–112, two per page, in order). The back cover (PDF p. 113) describes the five course components.

### Grammar spine

The workbook carries the Grade 6 grammar in explicit form: adjective order (size → colour → material); *comes from* vs *is made from*; *and* for adding, *but* for contrast; most/some/always/often/usually with materials; the full reflexive paradigm with the subject = object rule; should for advice against must for rules; the imperative as the instruction form; *because* for reasons; reported opinions with *think/say that … will …*; will/won't ordering and prediction; the comparative/superlative spelling system in two tables (-er/-est, -r/-st, y → i, consonant doubling, more/most, good/bad/far); regular/irregular past judgements; and dates with in/on/at. Its language-study spine is the richest of the three workbooks: -er/-or job endings, -tion nouns, homophones (to/too/two and seven further sets), silent letters, irregular plurals, abbreviations, dictionary entries (word / type / meaning), email structure, and the capital-letter rules.

### Honest negatives

- **A workbook**: its own texts (the waiter's day, the teacher's job, Hiba's family, the history of paper, cold remedies, Basim's laptop, the potato-chip story, Basim's homework, the planet biscuits, the Mother's Day text, the two present-buying texts) practise the Pupil's Book's syllabus rather than extending it; it cannot be used without the Pupil's Book and the audio.
- **No answer key**; checking is by listening, partner comparison or looking back at the Pupil's Book.
- Like the other activity books it has **no Arabic** — the English–Arabic dictionary lives in the Pupil's Book.
- Copy/scan notes: the file is complete (113/113 pages). The scan is greyscale and some light-grey panels print faintly (parts of the Unit 1 "I CAN" page and scattered word tiles are hard to read, though the page structure and task are clear). The cover's "Activity Book" label is washed out in this copy (see identity verdict). The planted-error items (the waiter's-day verb choices, Hiba's email, the web-word hunt, the anagram spellings) are deliberate teaching devices, as printed. No ISBN is printed; the back-cover barcode is a product code (TRG004224).

## Grade 6 — English for Iraq 6th Primary Teacher's Book (teacher's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title:** MATCH in substance, with a wording correction. The record calls this book "English for Iraq — Teacher's Guide"; the book itself is titled **Teacher's Book**, not Teacher's Guide, on its cover and in every running footer. It is the Grade 6 (6th Primary) teacher's companion to the Pupil's Book and Activity Book.
- **Title as printed:** *English for Iraq — 6th Primary — Teacher's Book* (front cover, PDF p. 1). The course is named "English for Iraq 6th Primary" throughout.
- **Authors as printed:** Sarah MacBurnie (sole named author, cover and imprint). The Editorial and Adaptation Committee is printed on the cover/imprint page: Hind Farooq Ali (Head of the English Curriculum), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha (Senior Education Specialist), Hanaa Adil Ali (Teacher of English), Batool Faeq Abdul Wahid (Senior Education Specialist), Faten Fakhri Khalaf (Primary Education Supervisor).
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (imprint, PDF p. 2). Issued for the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula.
- **Edition/year as printed:** "First edition © 2018 Garnet Publishing Ltd." (imprint, PDF p. 2). Production code printed on the imprint: IRQ18 TB 6thP.
- **ISBN as printed:** No ISBN printed — no ISBN appears on the cover, imprint or anywhere in the book's complete text layer.
- **PDF pages:** 200, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** Printed page = PDF page (offset 0). The printed folios in the footers match the PDF pagination throughout (Introduction opens on printed p. 5 = PDF p. 5; Contents, PDF p. 3, lists Unit 1 teaching notes at p. 33, Unit 8 at p. 186, appendices at pp. 194–198 — all confirmed at those PDF pages). All page references below are to the printed pages.
- **Text-layer character:** Born-digital with a complete, usable text layer — the only one of the nine Band B books with one. The book is bilingual by design: each English teaching-notes page is followed by an Arabic mirror page carrying the same notes in Arabic (the Introduction is also printed in Arabic first, PDF pp. 4–32 interleaved). No scanner wrapper; the file is the publisher's own layout.
- **Cast/design:** The notes teach the Pupil's Book (PB) and Activity Book (AB) lesson by lesson, printing the audio scripts (Tracks 1–113) inside the notes. Course characters in the pupil material: Wisam, Dania, Basim, Hiba and Ammar. The pupil course also includes a "Your dictionary" (English–Arabic wordlist at the end of the Pupil's Book), A2 posters, audio CD and a Teacher Resource File.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Introduction (pp. 5–32).** Aims, components and teaching guidance: how the course builds on Grades 1–5; advice on lesson planning, pair/group work, recycling language, assessment, homework and differentiation; how to teach listening (six text types), speaking, pronunciation, reading (phonics, audio-assisted and independent reading, high-frequency words) and writing (letter formation, punctuation, the apostrophe in contractions and possessives). The spelling method "Look, say, cover, write, check" is set out here: in Units 1–3 and 5–7 pupils learn four target words per unit, tested in Lesson 1 of the following unit. The Book Map (pp. 30–31) tabulates every unit's aims and core language. Four poster projects are flagged in the Pupil's Book ("P for Project!") in Units 2, 3, 6 and 7, and four model-based free-writing tasks sit in the Activity Book in Units 1, 3, 6 and 7.

**Unit 1 (teaching notes pp. 33–73; Pupil's Book pp. 6–21).** Family relationships and jobs. Vocabulary: family words (mother/mum, father/dad, grandparents, brother, sister, cousin, uncle, aunt/auntie, twin), a 19-word jobs set (engineer, IT programmer, farmer, teacher, housewife, shop assistant, nurse, bus driver, waiter, doctor, policeman, pilot, banker, cook, zoo keeper, officer, grocer, fisherman, artist), places of work (office, building site, restaurant, roads, hospital, oil refinery, home, farm) and adjectives (easy, hard, interesting, boring, dangerous, safe). Language: *How many cousins/brothers do you have? — I have two …*; *How old is …?*; *What's your dad's/mum's job?*; *Where does an engineer work? — He works in/on/at …* (third-person present simple with -s; prepositions in/on/at with places of work); daily routines, times of day and wh- questions; *When I grow up, I want to be a … because I like/I want to …*. Skills: a "My magazine" text about two families, a story about jobs (Lesson 8), and writing a paragraph about your own family following a model. Spelling words: aunt, uncle, easy, interesting.

**Unit 2 (pp. 74–95).** Materials and shopping. Vocabulary: materials (glass, wood/wooden, leather, metal, rubber, wool/woollen, cotton, denim), household objects, clothes, the departments and floors of a shopping mall, and opposite adjective pairs (soft/hard, cheap/expensive, long/short …) with cheaper/more expensive and the cheapest/the most expensive. Language: *What's/What are the … made of? — It's/They're made of …*; *comes from/made from* (a plant comes from a seed; a pot is made from clay); *It's too expensive/small …*; this/that, these/those contrasted with comparatives (*This is pretty. That is prettier. These are too short. Those are longer.*); describing objects (It is …, It has …, It's made from …); joining and contrasting with *and/but* (*I like tea and I like coffee / I like tea, but I don't like coffee*). The notes also revise present simple (routines) against present continuous (actions now), teach the homophones to/two/too, and set a "My magazine" blog by a hospital doctor and a project: design a school backpack. A rhyme about materials closes the unit. Spelling words: only, enough, many, usually.

**Unit 3 (pp. 96–115).** Health and the internet. Vocabulary: parts of the body (ear, leg, arm, mouth, teeth, hand, head, foot, eye, nose, toe, knee, shoulder, thumb, stomach, back), common ailments (a headache, a cough, a toothache, a sore throat, a temperature, a stomach ache, a cold, a rash, a broken arm, feel sick) and a large web-technology set (access, go online, search, key in, press, navigate, scroll up/down, click on, login, keyboard, website, webpage, screen, mouse, touchscreen, icon …). Language: *How are you feeling? — I don't feel well. I feel hot and I have a headache.*; *What's the matter?*; advice with should/shouldn't (*You should go to bed. You should take some medicine. You shouldn't eat so much cake.*); reflexive pronouns (*I hurt myself. She cut herself.*); rules with must/mustn't for staying safe online (*You must keep your password safe. You mustn't meet a person that you only know online.*). Skills: giving advice for health problems, reading an email from a friend, writing a paragraph about how to write an email and then an email to a friend following models, ordering and retelling the story "Helping my grandparents", and a healthy-habits project. Spelling words — the unit's "difficult words" with silent letters: knee, thumb, stomach, cough.

**Unit 4 (pp. 116–123).** Revision of Units 1–3. A four-lesson revision unit: a class challenge and spelling test (the Unit 3 words), review of jobs, body parts, illnesses, materials, prepositions with places of work, giving advice with should/shouldn't and "what you want to be"; short paragraph writing on Units 1–3 topics; a reading text about a computer mouse and an email to a friend.

**Unit 5 (pp. 124–143).** Change, inventions and the future. Language: the past/present/future contrast built on reflexives (*When I was younger my mum dressed me. Now I dress myself. In the future, I will buy my own clothes.*); predictions with will/won't (*In the future, I think/don't think that …; I think there will be/there won't be …*); change with getting + comparative (*Our lives are getting better. Computers are getting more intelligent.*); comparative spelling rules; dates with in/on (*When's your birthday? — It's in April. / It's on 7th April. / It's on Monday.*). Vocabulary: inventions and future technology (invention, century, robots, driverless cars) and the solar system (planet names, astronaut, spaceship, space travel). Skills: making a string telephone, words ending in -tion, correcting common spelling mistakes, reading about the solar system and space travel, and the homophones sun/son. Spelling words: easy/easier, heavy/heavier.

**Unit 6 (pp. 144–165).** Cooking and safety. Vocabulary: cooking verbs (add, spread, grate, peel, bake, mix, wash, slice), ingredients and utensils, quantity abbreviations in recipes, road-safety words (zebra crossing, seatbelt, traffic signal), dangerous things at home (matches, cleaning materials, electrical devices) and bike parts (brakes, handlebars, tyres, pedals, helmet). Language: asking and planning with some (*Can we make a pizza? We need some bread, tomatoes …*); *going to* for planned actions (*I'm going to slice the onions. I'm going to grate the cheese.*); past simple in short texts (*Yesterday, my mum baked a cake.*); imperatives and must for safety rules (*Spread the butter on the sandwich. You must be careful when you slice the onions. Don't play with matches. Put on your seatbelt. Cross the road on the zebra crossing. Keep knives … away from children.*); because to give reasons; comparative and superlative spelling rules. Skills: reading a recipe and ordering its instructions, describing a kitchen scene, the magazine article "Stay safe on your bike", writing notes to a brother or sister following a model, and a poster project on staying safe at home. Spelling words: should, shouldn't, must, mustn't.

**Unit 7 (pp. 166–185).** Parties and special days. Vocabulary: celebrations (Mother's Day, Teacher's Day, birthday, New Year), party items and a "To do" list (do the shopping, clean the house, do the cooking, buy/wrap presents, put up decorations, make a card, blow up balloons), presents described by material (leather bag, wooden picture frame, metal watch). Language: *going to* for future plans already agreed; the present continuous both for actions now and for future arrangements (*He's wrapping up presents. She's in the kitchen baking a cake. They're making a card.*); shops in a mall and prepositions of place (*Where's the watch shop? — It's on the first floor next to the pharmacy.*); adjective order in describing wrapping paper; should/must revision; correcting mistakes in present continuous sentences. Skills: reading about Mother's Day, following instructions to make a card and writing special-day messages, a story to read/order/retell, and a "special day" card poster project. Spelling words (materials): woollen, wooden, leather, metal.

**Unit 8 (pp. 186–193).** Revision of Units 5–7. A four-lesson revision unit: class challenge and spelling test (the Unit 7 words), review of the three units' vocabulary and language, listening/reading and answering questions about Teacher's Day, writing a description of a picture following a model, reading an email to grandparents, a spelling-game conversation, and the song "When I grow up" to close the course.

**Appendices (pp. 194–200).** Teacher Resource File Transcript (pp. 194–196): the key language of every unit in script form plus a bank of classroom-instruction phrases. Photocopiable Spelling Grids (p. 197): the four-word test grids for Units 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7. Photocopiable Game Circles (pp. 198–199): Game circles A and B. A ruled Notes page (p. 200) ends the book.

### Grammar spine

Consolidation year: the present simple (including third-person -s and does-questions) and the present continuous (actions now, then future arrangements) are revised and contrasted; *going to* for plans and *will/won't* for predictions carry the future; the past simple appears in stories and short texts (*Yesterday, my mum baked a cake*) and in the past/present/future contrast of Unit 5, but is never set out as a paradigm. Also: comparatives and superlatives with their spelling rules; should/shouldn't for advice; must/mustn't and imperatives for rules; reflexive pronouns; prepositions in/on/at for place and in/on for time; adjective order before nouns; made of/made from/comes from; this/that/these/those; joining with and/but and giving reasons with because; possessive 's in family questions (*What's your dad's job?*).

### Honest negatives

- **No present perfect, no passive, no conditionals, no reported speech** anywhere in the Grade 6 course as documented by this book.
- **The past simple is used but not taught as a system**: no verb tables, no regular/irregular lists, no past-tense question/negative forms are presented in the teaching notes; past forms occur inside stories, examples and the Unit 5 time contrast.
- **No phonics programme**: pronunciation work is limited to word stress, the -tion ending, homophone pairs (to/two/too, sun/son) and the spelling strand (four high-frequency or "difficult" words per unit, tested cumulatively); silent letters are flagged word by word (knee, thumb).
- The Teacher's Book contains **no answer material beyond the answers embedded in the lesson notes**, no tests other than the spelling tests and revision-unit challenges, and no pupil-facing text of its own — everything pupil-facing lives in the Pupil's Book and Activity Book it keys to.
- Copy character: this file is clean and complete (born-digital, full text layer). The only misprint-level slip spotted is in the Unit 8 Lesson 1 notes, where the spelling-test instruction breaks off mid-word ("Say the first word, woolle…") at a page turn — recorded as printed.

## Grade 7 — English for Iraq, 1st Intermediate Student's Book (student's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Student's Book"):** MATCH — the file is the genuine current Garnet student's book for the first intermediate year (Iraqi Grade 7).
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *English for Iraq — 1st Intermediate Student's Book*, under the Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow and Paul Drury. Editorial and Adaptation Committee: Malek Mohamed Juda (Head of the English Curriculum), Adil Ali Moussa, Muayyad Naji Ahmed, Ramzi Nouri Abdullah. Revision Committee: Hind Farooq Ali (Head), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha, Hanaa Adil Ali, Batool Faiyq Abdul-Wahid, Ahmed Saadoon Azeez, Khaldon Wahhab Allawi.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd. (imprint: Garnet Education), 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, PDF p. 100):** "First edition © 2014 Garnet Publishing Ltd. Revised edition © 2021 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ21 SB 1stI". This copy is the 2021 revised edition.
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — none appears on the title page or the imprint page.
- **PDF pages:** 100, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** zero — printed page = PDF page throughout the numbered book (Book map is printed pp. 2–3 = PDF pp. 2–3; the last numbered page, Grammar reference p. 99, is PDF p. 99). PDF p. 1 is the unnumbered title page and PDF p. 100 the unnumbered imprint page.
- **Text-layer character:** none — the PDF is page images only (pdftotext returns nothing). Read whole from 4-up contact sheets of 110 DPI renders, every sheet inspected.
- **Copy marks:** a small third-party website watermark stamp (an Arabic site logo) is printed over some pages; it is a distribution stamp on the scan, not part of the book. The cast is a mixed Iraqi school group (Ahmed, Faisal, Rasha, Huda, Muna, Bassem, Adel, Maysam, Laith, Rawan) plus recurring visitors (Richard, Simon, Karen, Yuki).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Book map (printed pp. 2–3).** A full syllabus spread: eight units with lexical areas, communicative activities, language-learning activities, grammatical areas and pronunciation work for each, and the four projects named (Project 1: Me and my holidays; 2: My moods and feelings; 3: My experiences; 4: How to stay safe).

**Unit 1 — My spare time (pp. 4–15).** Lesson 1 The holidays: past-simple question-and-answer exchanges about holiday activities (swimming, camping, fishing, shopping, cooking, the zoo, the museum). Lesson 2 Richard in Iraq: describing photographs of Iraq (football, the Citadel in Erbil, Hatra, Ur, a traditional market, the marshes in the south) with opinion adjectives (amazing, boring, exciting, fantastic, great, huge). Lesson 3 You were watching the flamingos …: a Mariam-and-Khalid dialogue that presents the past continuous against the past simple (background, interrupted and completed actions). Lesson 4 Whose scrapbooks are these?: spare-time interests and collections, possessive *'s*, and *like/enjoy/be interested in + -ing*. Lesson 5 How often?: the frequency adverbs *always, usually, often, sometimes, never* as pie-chart proportions, with the position rule (after *be*, before other verbs). Lesson 6 Bassem's scrapbook: completing a conversation (question formation) and the tongue-twister "She sells seashells". Lesson 7 A special day: planning and recounting a special day in the past simple. Lesson 8 A letter from Australia: Karen's letter from Sydney, worked on for reference words (*back, we, one, who, everyone, some*). Round up (p. 12), Story time "My new friend, the swift" (p. 13 — rescuing an injured swift), Project 1 (pp. 14–15).

**Unit 2 — Dates and seasons (pp. 16–25).** Lesson 1 Our four seasons: weather words (cold, cool, hot, rain, snow, sunny, warm, windy), England's seasons, and a London weather forecast presenting *will* with time phrases. Lesson 2 What will we do?: a grandfather–grandson conversation of *will* questions about tomorrow in a snowy country. Lesson 3 The months of the year: a months poem, capital letters for months, and an England temperature table (coldest/hottest month). Lesson 4 What's the date?: the "Thirty days has September" rhyme, the full ordinal set first–thirty-first, saying and writing dates, and Iraqi Armed Forces Day (6th January). Lesson 5 Life in Boronuk: a reading text about extreme cold in a Russian town (temperatures to −67°C, frozen ink, petrol and phone batteries). Lesson 6 Different schools: Yassir explains Iraq's semesters and moving festivals (Eid al-Fitr) against Simon's England terms. Lesson 7 Bobsleighing: the fastest winter sport: a question-and-answer factual text (speeds to a printed world record of 201 km/h). Lesson 8 Eid al-Fitr: a picture-sequenced explanation of Ramadan and the festival (fast, dawn, dusk, festival, break our fast). Round up (p. 24) and Story time "New Year's Day in London" (p. 25 — a snow-blinded ride on the London Eye).

**Unit 3 — What have you done today? (pp. 26–37).** Lesson 1 I've finished my work: a chores song that introduces the present perfect (*have/has +* past participle, for things done "just now, today, this week"). Lesson 2 Many hands make light work: a text on chores at home, Japanese schoolchildren cleaning their classrooms and Rwanda's monthly community cleaning. Lesson 3 Good Gwen and Lazy Len: present perfect questions and short answers in two contrasting dialogues. Lesson 4 The Hippo Roller: an invention text (the problem / the idea / the solution) about carrying water. Lesson 5 What's the matter?: matching feelings (angry, upset, happy, excited, sad, frightened, worried) to present perfect explanations. Lesson 6 Helping Gran: a story in which Noor helps her grandmother make a video call and discovers her medal for mathematics. Lesson 7 The hungry fox: a fable carrying the adverbs-of-manner box (*-ly*, *y → i*, irregular *fast/well*). Lesson 8 Once upon a time …: ordering the pictures of the boy who cried wolf. Round up (p. 34), Story time "The bear that wasn't a bear" (p. 35), Project 2 My moods and feelings (pp. 36–37).

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 38–41).** Four skills-review lessons only: Lesson 1 Lazy Len's day (a vocabulary tip on grouping words — nouns/adjectives/adverbs — manner adverbs in sentences, and the Word chains game). Lesson 2 Khalid's birthday present (conversation completion; a punctuation box on the exclamation mark; Speaking tip 2 on acting feelings). Lesson 3 The monkey in the café (Reading tip 1: working out meanings from context, practised on a café story). Lesson 4 Writing a story (improving a flat picture story; Writing tip 3: use adjectives and adverbs). No new grammar and no Round up, Story time or Project in this unit.

**Unit 5 — Other countries (pp. 42–53).** Lesson 1 Other countries: identifying France, India, Japan and Oman from photographs and four young people (Emile, Sultana, Raji, Yuki) by origin. Lesson 2 Our world: a labelled world map and *Have you ever been to …?*, with the *gone to / been to* distinction. Lesson 3 Granny's box of memories: a story of travel souvenirs carrying the book's explicit present-perfect-vs-past-simple contrast box. Lesson 4 We've been everywhere: a travel song in the present perfect. Lessons 5–6 Four countries (Parts A and B): parallel factual texts on India and Oman, then Japan and France (size, population, farming, industry, visitors). Lesson 7 Things to do in Iraq: the Hawizeh Marshes, masgouf, the ancient city of Hatra and the Baghdad Battery, all framed as *Have you visited/eaten/seen/heard …?* Lesson 8 Have you ever been to Indonesia?: a photo tour (ape, pepper/coffee/rubber, beaches, temples, the Istiqlal Mosque in Jakarta, volcanoes, bird of paradise). Round up (p. 50) adds comparatives and superlatives for comparing countries and questions with a preposition at the end ("Which countries have you been to?"). Story time "Carrie in the jungle" (p. 51 — an eye-controlled computer game) and Project 3 My experiences (pp. 52–53).

**Unit 6 — Friends abroad (pp. 54–63).** Lesson 1 A Japanese penfriend: Yuki Ono's letter from Soma, Honshu, to Fuad (personal description, Japan's geography and weather, invitations). Lesson 2 Linking things: the linking words *and, but, or, because, as well as*. Lesson 3 Samar's penfriend: completing a conversation about Judy, a penfriend in Canada (nationalities). Lesson 4 Which one is Yuki?: reading a written self-description to identify a person at an airport arrivals hall. Lesson 5 Fuad meets Yuki: formal introductions ("How do you do?") and a narrative of the journey (driving on the left, kimono, signs in Japanese). Lessons 6–7 Fuad's first meal in Japan (Parts A and B): a picture dialogue on Japanese customs (kneeling to eat, chopsticks, raw fish) contrasted with Iraq. Lesson 8 A postcard from Fuad: Fuad's postcard from Japan to Salih in Basra, judged for how feelings show in writing. Round up (p. 62) covers describing people and introduces the future continuous inside a three-way continuous-tense comparison ("At this time on Thursday, I will be playing basketball"). Story time "A wolf in the forest" (p. 63).

**Unit 7 — Danger! (pp. 64–75).** Lesson 1 Instructions: matching imperatives to pictures (recipes, signs, notices). Lesson 2 Warnings to children: a danger-filled kitchen picture with reflexive pronouns ("She might burn herself") and a danger vocabulary set (poisoning, drowning, cuts, scalds, choking, burns, falls). Lesson 3 Watch out!: reading safety posters ("DON'T LET ACCIDENTS HAPPEN IN YOUR HOME!") and working out new words. Lesson 4 Going fishing: a dialogue in Mazin's fishing shop in Um Qasr that runs on first conditionals ("If the boat sinks, we'll swim"). Lesson 5 If you do that …: completing the safety poem "DON'T WORRY!" and the if-sentences rule box (present tense in the if-clause, *will +* infinitive in the result clause, either clause first). Lesson 6 What will we do if …?: a New Zealand camping conversation of *What will we do if …?* questions. Lesson 7 Danger!: completing a conversation about a toddler and a hot bath. Lesson 8 Road safety: spotting dangers in street pictures (handlebars, cyclist, pavement, zebra crossing, seat belt) and comparing England's streets with Iraq's. Round up (p. 72), Story time "Danger at sea" (p. 73), Project 4 How to stay safe (pp. 74–75).

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 76–79).** Four review lessons: Lesson 1 Pronunciation (rising intonation for Yes/No questions, falling for answers; building longer conversations). Lesson 2 Penfriends (Reading tip 2: scanning for names, ages, countries; four penfriend adverts — Rona, Hans, Josh, Nasreen). Lesson 3 Keep listening! (Listening tip 1: don't stop listening; note-taking on good and bad points about food, cars, TV/internet and mobile phones). Lesson 4 A different kind of holiday (Reading tip 3: predict from title and pictures, keep reading; a cliff-fall story set in Amadiya in Duhok, finished in the Activity Book).

**Transcripts (pp. 80–96) and Grammar and functions reference (pp. 97–99).** Full transcripts for every audio item, including items with no Student's Book page of their own (e.g. Unit 3 Extra activities, Unit 5 Lesson 9 Round up listening). The reference summarises, by unit: present/past simple and past continuous with a past-forms verb table (Unit 1); future with *will* and time phrases (Unit 2); present perfect and adverbs of manner (Unit 3); present perfect with *ever/never* and comparatives/superlatives (Unit 5); linking words and the future continuous (Unit 6); imperatives, reflexive pronouns and the first conditional (Unit 7). Imprint and photography credits close the book (PDF p. 100).

### Grammar spine

Present simple and past simple (Unit 1, as revision with a full past-forms table) → past continuous against past simple (Unit 1) → possessive *'s* and *like/enjoy + -ing* (Unit 1) → frequency adverbs and their position (Unit 1) → future with *will* and time phrases (Unit 2) → the present perfect for recent completion (Unit 3) → adverbs of manner (Unit 3) → present perfect with *ever/never* for life experience, contrasted with the past simple, plus *been to / gone to* (Unit 5) → comparatives and superlatives (Unit 5) → linking words *and/but/or/because/as well as* (Unit 6) → the future continuous, introduced only in the Unit 6 Round up (Unit 6) → imperatives, reflexive pronouns and the first conditional (Unit 7).

### Honest negatives

- **No passive voice anywhere, no reported speech, no second or third conditionals, no present perfect continuous, no modal system beyond *can* and *will/would* in fixed phrases, and no relative clauses.** The future continuous is named and exemplified in one Round up box and the reference but is never practised in a lesson.
- **No word list or glossary** — unlike the primary course, this book has no end-of-book vocabulary list; vocabulary is carried by the Book map and the Activity Book.
- The Grammar and functions reference has **no entries for Units 4 and 8** (review units) — as printed, not a copy defect.
- Factual content is as printed and dated in places (e.g. the bobsleigh world record of 201 km/h, India's population as "about one thousand million"); recorded here as printed, not corrected.
- The scan is complete and clean: all 100 pages present, in order, with no missing or duplicated pages; the only copy marks are the small third-party site watermark stamps noted above.

## Grade 7 — English for Iraq, 1st Intermediate Activity Book (activity book / workbook)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Activity Book"):** MATCH — the genuine companion workbook to the 1st Intermediate Student's Book.
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *English for Iraq — 1st Intermediate Activity Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula; imprint Garnet Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow and Paul Drury; the imprint additionally thanks Donna Bailey for her contribution to this work. Editorial and Adaptation Committee and Revision Committee as in the Student's Book.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, printed p. 2):** "First edition © 2014 Garnet Publishing Ltd. Revised edition © 2021 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ21 AB 1stI".
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — none on the title page or imprint page.
- **PDF pages:** 128, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** zero — the imprint is printed p. 2 = PDF p. 2, the "This book belongs to" page is printed p. 3 = PDF p. 3, and the last page, "Notes", is printed p. 128 = PDF p. 128. PDF p. 1 is the unnumbered title page.
- **Text-layer character:** none — page images only; read whole from 4-up contact sheets. The book is printed in black and white (greyscale photographs), unlike the colour Student's Book.
- **Copy marks:** the same small third-party website watermark stamp seen in the Student's Book appears on some pages; a scan stamp, not part of the book.

### Unit-by-unit summary

The workbook mirrors the Student's Book lesson by lesson (the Student's Book headers cite these as "AB" pages), with written exercises, listening tasks keyed to the same audio, puzzles (wordsearches, word trees, crosswords), pair-work tables, and its own teaching boxes and tips, which sometimes carry the only explicit statement of a rule.

**Unit 1 — My spare time (printed pp. 4–21).** Holiday yes/no survey and paragraph writing with Writing tip 1 (check spelling, punctuation, wrong and missing words, then rewrite); Richard-in-Iraq listening notes, vocabulary definitions (ancient, citadel, marsh, ziggurat, bright) and sentence ordering; past-form verb tables by regular/irregular/no-change type and a past-simple wordsearch; the box "Two ways of talking about the past" (past simple vs past continuous) with a mixed-tense paragraph completion; adjective stress marking; possessive pronouns (*mine, yours, hers, ours, theirs*) and the box "Talking about your interests" (gerunds vs nouns after *like/enjoy/be interested in*); the frequency-adverb rule completed from Nader's text, a guessing game and pie-chart sentence building; conversation completion (Hanaa and Rafel, question forms); special-day note tables; a workbook-only reading text about Ahmed visiting Faisal in New York (Central Park); reconstructing Karen's six questions and writing a reply letter; a Round up on adjective spelling and the box "Joining sentences with and, but, or"; Story time questions and story notes on the swift.

**Unit 2 — Dates and seasons (pp. 22–39).** Weather-word matching and a noun/verb/adjective word-building table (snow/snowy, rain/rainy); *will/won't* weather sentences about Iraq; listening picture-ordering for the grandfather conversation; the box "Using commas in lists"; capital-letter checks and a word-tree puzzle; the box "Spelling" for ordinals (five–fifth, eight–eighth, twelve–twelfth, twenty–twentieth, forty–fortieth), a days-in-month table from the rhyme, and listening birthdays written as dates; dates written in words from a July calendar; Boronuk problem pictures and *won't* sentences; capital letters and commas corrected in a paragraph about Iraqi Armed Forces Day; completing a paragraph on the Iraqi school year; word sorting into Holidays/Months/Weather/Seasons/How often; bobsleigh vocabulary multiple choice and discussion; Eid al-Fitr vocabulary and short answers with Speaking tip 1 (explaining things clearly); Writing tip 2 — Make a plan, for a two-paragraph letter about Eid; a Round up of rhyming-word spelling, word order and dates; Story time questions on New Year's Day in London.

**Unit 3 — What have you done today? (pp. 40–57).** A full infinitive/past tense/past participle table (regular cook–sweep; irregular see–make) and sorting past participles by the /t/, /ɪd/, /d/ endings; present perfect picture sentences and participle gap-fills; correcting false sentences about the Japan/Rwanda chores article; a chores collocation set (lay the table, sweep the floor, make the bed, tidy your room, throw away the rubbish); writing present perfect questions and answers from a party-clean-up picture; the Hippo Roller as a combining-inventions drawing task, problem/solution lists, and the workbook-only box "Making suggestions" (*Why don't we …? / We could … / Let's …*); a feelings wordsearch; matching story words to definitions (patiently, giggled, anxious, nervous, embarrassed, successful) and writing video-call instructions for grandparents; finding and forming adverbs of manner; Student A/B "Read and talk" conversation frames (lost property, offers of help); ordering the crying-wolf pictures, completing the fable text, and writing an own lying story; a Round up converting past-time sentences into the present perfect; Story time questions on the bear story.

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 58–61).** Rewriting sentences with manner and frequency adverbs; sorting twenty words into Weather/School/Calendar/Animals; punctuating direct speech; placing social expressions (Never mind. / Hang on! / How do you do? / That's very kind of you.); the monkey-in-the-café vocabulary in sentences and choosing meanings of highlighted words from context (exhausted, atlas, thoroughly, furious, replied); and finishing Yassir's improved snake story. **Extra activities for Units 1–4 follow at pp. 62–65** (labelled "E"): favourite-meal writing and possessives (Unit 1); a workbook-only reading text, Majid's holiday in England (Unit 2); punctuation, the Len-and-his-father listening, and word-choice exercises (Unit 3); a gap-fill crocodile-in-New-York story to make more interesting, plus story beginnings (Unit 4).

**Unit 5 — Other countries (pp. 66–83).** Naming countries from landmark photographs (Mt Fuji, the Louvre, the Taj Mahal, Singapore) and odd-one-out sets; a two-minute country-listing race by region; the *I've been to …* chain game and a both/never partner table; verb-form tables and Granny's-box questions, including identifying further souvenirs; the box "Making questions" (word order, questions ending in prepositions) with write-the-question exercises; a feelings-and-intonation comparison of two Helen conversations; note-tables and paragraph writing on India/Oman and Japan/France; the box "Comparatives and superlatives" with a full adjective table (cold–coldest to rainy) and data-based comparison questions (population, area, July temperature for Oman, Japan, India, France) and Iraq-vs-England comparisons; visitor questions for Iraq (Hatra, the marshes) and recommendations; completing a description of Indonesia from a fact box (Mount Rinjani, the ape "with long, red hair"); a Round up listening (Jassim and Rana's food shopping) and two paragraphs about Iraq; alphabetical ordering of country names; Story time questions on Carrie in the jungle.

**Unit 6 — Friends abroad (pp. 84–101).** Matching Yuki's letter paragraphs to headings and note-taking; the box "Nationalities" (suffixes *-i, -ian/-an/-n, -ish*; irregular Dutch and Thai) with a 24-country formation table; drafting a reply letter to Yuki through the full plan–draft–check–final-copy cycle; completing and joining sentences with *and, but, or, because, as well as*; a KWL table for a chosen penfriend country (worked example: Mexico); listening completion of Fuad's travel plans and note-correction; the workbook's own grammar box "The future continuous tense" (rule: *will + be +* verb-*ing*) with personal questions and an airport self-description; finding synonyms in the Fuad-meets-Yuki text; labelling Yuki's family and comparing them (comparatives and superlatives); Japan/Iraq custom tables with *always/sometimes/never* and rewriting over-general statements ("People in Japan sometimes eat raw fish"); completing a dolma conversation for Yuki's return visit; postcard analysis and Writing tip 4 (make a postcard interesting, use feelings), writing a postcard from Iraq; a Round up listening (Jim and Tom on Italy), describing two men from drawings, and future continuous sentences; Story time correction work on A wolf in the forest.

**Unit 7 — Danger! (pp. 102–119).** The box "Imperatives for instructions, orders and warnings" (base form; negative *Don't/Never*) and sorting imperatives by speaker (parents/teacher/self); matching dangers to objects, completing the reflexive pronoun table (*-self/-selves*) and matching reflexive sentences to pictures; the box "Always and never for warnings" with poster conversations; fishing vocabulary photographs (compass, life jacket, oars, motor) and answering Mazin's conditional questions in writing; the "DON'T WORRY!" poem completed, first-aid situation notes, conditional verb-form drills and a conversation completion; building *What will we do if …?* conversations both ways (question-writing from the New Zealand dialogue, phrase-box dialogues, matching clause halves); the Student A/B accident conversation frames; matching speech bubbles to danger types and road-safety warnings to pictures; the lost-boy listening (Fareed) with note questions; a Round up listening (Nader's family drive) with *-self* completions; Story time questions on Danger at sea.

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 120–123).** Definition-to-word vocabulary (magazine, handlebars, life jacket, oars, suit, medicine); question word-order puzzles including a question with a final preposition; writing Khalid's questions from Ahmed's answers; the penfriend scanning table (name, girl/boy, age, country) and writing a reply to an advert; a mixed-tense verb completion (present/past simple, present continuous, past continuous, present perfect, reflexive *ourselves*) and joining sentences with linking words; Reading tip 4 (information may need to be worked out, not found word-for-word) and the workbook-only conclusion of the Duhok cliff story — the narrator, Mr Ammaar, wakes in hospital, is nursed by a Kurdish nurse who speaks Arabic, and resolves to learn her language. **Extra activities for Units 5–8 close the book at pp. 124–127**: past participle sentences and a workbook-only fact text on Spain with a "Facts about Spain" table (Unit 5); a reading text about Amal's American penfriend Kate visiting Iraq (Unit 6); homophone and word-order exercises with first conditional completions (Unit 7); a verb-form story completion, "Special offer! Cheap trips to India!" (Unit 8). A blank "Notes" page is printed p. 128.

### Grammar spine (as practised and boxed in this workbook)

Past simple vs past continuous (Unit 1 box) → possessive pronouns and gerunds after *like/enjoy* (Unit 1) → frequency adverb position (Unit 1) → joining sentences with *and/but/or* (Unit 1 Round up) → *will/won't* (Unit 2) → commas in lists, capitals, ordinal spelling (Unit 2) → present perfect with full participle tables and /t/ /ɪd/ /d/ endings (Unit 3) → adverbs of manner (Unit 3) → making suggestions (Unit 3) → punctuation of speech (Units 3–4) → question formation incl. final prepositions (Unit 5) → comparatives and superlatives (Unit 5) → nationality adjectives (Unit 6) → linking words *and/but/or/because/as well as* (Unit 6) → the future continuous, given its rule box here in the workbook (Unit 6) → imperatives and reflexive pronouns (Unit 7) → *always/never* warnings (Unit 7) → the first conditional (Unit 7).

## Grade 7 — English for Iraq, 1st Intermediate Teacher's Book (teacher's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Teacher's Guide"):** MATCH in substance — the genuine Garnet teacher's book for the first intermediate year; the book calls itself "Teacher's Book", not "Teacher's Guide".
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 3):** *English for Iraq — 1st Intermediate Teacher's Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education; imprint Garnet Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill and Peter Snow. Editorial and Adaptation Committee (title page and back cover): Malek Mohamed Juda (Head of the English Curriculum), Adil Ali Moussa (Senior Education Specialist), Muayyad Naji Ahmed (Head of the English Department in the Open Education College), Ramzi Noori Abdullah (Senior Education Specialist). Short author biographies are printed on the imprint page (O'Neill: Dublin graduate, Head of English in Dar-es-Salaam and later Bahrain, MA in Applied Linguistics at Essex in 1972, then BBC *English by Radio and Television*; Snow: modern languages at London University, taught in Germany and the Netherlands, then the BBC).
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, PDF p. 4):** "First edition, copyright © 2014 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ14 TB 1stI". The back cover is dated 2014. **This file is the original 2014 edition, not the 2021 revised edition** of the Student's/Activity Books in this set — content corresponds lesson-for-lesson, but a checker should note the edition difference.
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — the imprint page carries none; the back cover carries a barcode with the product code "EKG002101" as printed, which is not a 978 ISBN.
- **PDF pages:** 177, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** the book's own pages are folioed 1–172 in the footer ("English for Iraq Teacher's Book 1st Intermediate: <section>"), and **PDF page = printed page + 4** throughout (printed p. 1 = PDF p. 5; printed p. 172, the first Notes page, = PDF p. 176). The four pages before printed p. 1 are: PDF p. 1, a wrapper page added by the scanning website (see below); PDF p. 2, the book's front cover; PDF p. 3, the title page; PDF p. 4, the imprint page. PDF p. 177 is the book's back cover.
- **Text-layer character:** the embedded text layer is only the scanning website's repeated Arabic header/footer (its name and a copyright line) — the book itself is image-only and was read whole from 4-up contact sheets.
- **⚠️ Third-party scanner wrapper (as the library checklist warned):** this copy comes from an Iraqi education website (its name, in Arabic, is "Education for Iraq"). The site (a) adds its own cover page as PDF p. 1 — an Arabic page presenting "the English teacher's guide for the first intermediate grade, complete from cover to cover, exclusively" on the site, dedicating the reward of the work to the martyrs of Iraq and in particular to the scanner's brother, and asserting the site's copyright while permitting republication only without removing its publishing-rights marks; (b) stamps its name at the top and a copyright line at the bottom of **every** scanned page; (c) overlays a large translucent version of its circular logo across the book's front cover (PDF p. 2); and (d) inserts a full-page copy of its logo as printed p. 107 (PDF p. 111, between the Unit 5 and Unit 6 sections) and stamps the same logo onto the Unit 6 word-list page (printed p. 125). None of this is part of the Garnet book; the book's own pages are otherwise complete and in order underneath the stamps.

### Structure and unit-by-unit summary

**Introduction (printed pp. 1–25).** Section A, "About English for Iraq, Intermediate stage": the components (Student's Book, Activity Book, audio CD), how the books interlock, exercise types, the year's organisation — 74 lessons in eight units; core units (1–3, 5–7) have ten lessons, revision Units 4 and 8 have seven; Unit 1 partly revises the previous year; Lesson 10 is extensive reading (Story time) and test marking; the AB Extra activities can be set as tests; each unit ends with a Word list of testable and non-testable (*) vocabulary — plus a list of the six reading-comprehension passages recommended for assessment, and the abbreviations used (SB5 = Student's Book page 5, TP L1 = Teaching Procedure Listening 1, etc.). Section B, "Overview of main grammatical areas" (pp. 5–8): a reference table of every grammar and punctuation area in the book with the unit of first occurrence — adjectives and comparison; adverbs of frequency (Unit 1) and manner (Unit 3); adverbial phrases; conjunctions and link words including *as well as* (Unit 6); determiners and articles; *some/any*; gerunds; imperatives including *always/never* forms (Unit 7); infinitives after *want* and *would like*; modals and auxiliaries (*have to*, *can*); nouns and plurals; possessives; prepositions; pronouns including reflexives (Unit 7); punctuation (apostrophe, capitals, commas, exclamation mark, full stop, question mark); question forms including *How long/How often* (Unit 1); and tenses — present simple, present continuous (including for future arrangements), present perfect (Units 3 and 5), past simple, past continuous, future with *will* (Unit 2), future continuous (Unit 6), and Type 1 conditionals (Unit 7). Section C, "Advice to teachers" (pp. 9–12): the classroom (furniture, wall pictures, display area, book corner, music, extra materials, the scrapbook supplies list), pair and group work, mixed abilities, lesson planning (planning ahead; the A–D lesson pattern), classroom language, using Arabic (English first; demonstration better than explanation; Arabic mainly for checking understanding), and assessment (ongoing assessment; AB marks). Section D, "Teaching techniques and procedures" (pp. 13–25): the integrated-skills approach; listening objectives and materials (the two songs — Unit 3 "I've finished my work" and Unit 5 "We've been everywhere" — and the rhymes) with procedures TP L1 (songs) and TP L2 (task-listening); speaking objectives and TP S1/S2; vocabulary objectives, testable vocabulary and the word-list system, TP V1 (revision/development) and TP V2 (working out meaning from context: synonyms, antonyms, explanations, generic terms, contextual clues, word formation, general knowledge), vocabulary-quiz suggestions; reading text types and objectives (predicting, skimming, scanning, intensive and extensive reading) with TP R1–R4 (including the Story time procedure and its feedback chart); writing objectives and the correction-symbol system (P punctuation, G grammar, Sp spelling, ? unclear), TP W1/W2; dictation (TP D1 — there are no dictation exercises in the book; teachers may use SB/AB sentences); and language awareness/spelling and punctuation work.

Each unit's notes then follow a fixed pattern: a **Unit Summary** table (lesson by lesson: materials, topic area, activities, language), **Unit objectives** by skill, step-by-step **lesson notes** with Objectives/Language/Vocabulary boxes, full **transcripts** of the audio, **answers** to Student's Book and Activity Book tasks, Extra activities answers, and a closing **UNIT WORD LIST** (alphabetical within the unit, each item keyed to its lesson, non-testable items starred).

**Unit 1 — My spare time (notes pp. 26–43).** Lessons: holiday conversations (past simple questions and short answers); Richard in Iraq (adjectives); AB tense tables and wordsearch (past simple and past continuous forms, stressed syllables); spare-time activities and the scrapbook project launch (possessive *'s*, possessive pronouns); *How often?* and the weekly routine (present tense questions, adverbs of frequency); Rashid's scrapbook conversation and the sea-shells tongue-twister (present simple); making the scrapbook from recorded and written instructions; Karen's letter from Australia (letter conventions, pronoun reference, question formation); Round up; Story time "The birdwatcher" (Muna and the yellow-billed stork); Extra activities answers including the Sara holiday listening; Unit 1 word list.

**Unit 2 — Dates and seasons (pp. 44–60).** Lessons 1–2: seasons and a weather forecast (*will* future; position of time adverbials). Lesson 3: months, abbreviations, *It (rains) in (April)*, commas in lists, the weather rhyme. Lessons 4–5: "Thirty days has September", ordinals, spelling of abbreviations, dates and birthdays. Lessons 6–7: the British and Iraqi school years compared (present simple; capital letters), writing about the Iraqi school year, classifying vocabulary. Lesson 8: Eid al-Fitr — Yassir's father explains Ramadan and Eid to Simon from England (full transcript), note-taking, Speaking tip 1, and writing a letter about Eid (Writing tip 2). Lesson 9 Round up; Lesson 10 Story time "New Year's Day in London" (Yassir and Halla on the London Eye); Unit 2 word list. Project: The weather in Iraq and England.

**Unit 3 — What have you done today? (pp. 62–79).** The present perfect unit: Lessons 1–2 Good Gwen and Lazy Len (regular past participles; the Archer family; pocket money); Lesson 3 the song "I've finished my work" and participle pronunciation (/t/, /ɪd/, /d/); Lesson 4 an AB-only listening lesson (six mini-conversations; note-taking); Lesson 5 "What's the matter?" (irregular participles; feelings adjectives; *just*); Lesson 6 Mad Mack and Mack's robot (comic strip; present perfect); Lesson 7 The hungry fox (Aesop; adverbs of manner; making conversations); Lesson 8 "Once upon a time …" — the shepherd who cried wolf, listened to, sequenced, retold and completed in writing; Lesson 9 Round up; Lesson 10 Story time "Khalid and the key"; Unit 3 word list (participles marked (pp)). Project: Moods and feelings.

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 80–89).** Seven lessons: Lazy Len's day (vocabulary grouping tip; Word chain game; adverb position); Khalid's birthday present (exclamation marks; pronunciation; the Whispering game); The monkey in the café (working out meanings; everyday expressions); story writing (reading and improving Yassir's desert story with Writing tip 3, completing the crocodile story, writing an own story); Extra activities; Unit 4 word list. The unit's stated purpose is recycling Units 1–3 for the end-of-semester examination.

**Unit 5 — Other countries (pp. 90–106).** Lesson 1: country names and *X comes from …*; present perfect with *ever/never*; a class survey. Lesson 2: the Big Bill cartoon (present perfect contrasted with past simple; *Where do the (oranges) come from?*). Lesson 3: questionnaire and "tree" conversations; verb tables. Lesson 4: the song "We've been everywhere" and questions ending with a preposition; starting the World tourist sights project. Lessons 5–6: four country texts (India, Oman, Japan, France) with information tables; comparatives and superlatives in full (add -er, double consonant, change y to i, *more/most*); comparing Iraq and England from a data table (area in square kilometres, "one point nine million"). Lesson 7: the Indonesia radio advertisement. Lesson 8: revision and project work. Lesson 9 Round up (with the Jassim and Rana food-shopping transcript). Lesson 10 Story time "Where have you been?" (Shatha's computer game; the AB wordsearch hides "Pakistan", and a "FACTS ABOUT SPAIN" panel is answered in the notes); Unit 5 word list.

**Unit 6 — Friends abroad (pp. 108–125).** Lesson 1: Yuki Ono's letter from Japan (paragraph organisation). Lesson 2: link words (*and, but, because, or, as well as*) and forming nationality adjectives; drafting a reply through the full writing process (plan, draft, improve, final copy). Lesson 3: Samar's pen-friend Judy in Canada. Lessons 4–5: "Which one is Yuki?" and Fuad meets Yuki — identifying people from descriptions, notes on two long listening conversations (Fuad's Japan trip: Iraqi Airways, twelve days, a seven-hour flight), and the future continuous presented from "I will be wearing a T-shirt" and used for Fuad imagining Yuki at the airport; formal introductions at Tokyo airport (Mr and Mrs Ono; *How do you do?*). Lesson 6: Fuad's first meal in Japan (kneeling, chopsticks, raw fish; adverbs of frequency in comparisons of Japanese and Iraqi customs). Lesson 7: Fuad's postcard and Mustafa's postcard from France (present perfect and past simple; Writing tip 4). Lesson 8 revision; Lesson 9 Round up with a Jim-and-Tom listening; Lesson 10 Story time "A wolf in the forest". Unit 6 word list (the site's logo is stamped across the bottom of this page in this copy — the list itself is complete above it).

**Unit 7 — Danger! (pp. 126–142).** Lesson 1 Instructions (imperatives including negatives with *Don't/Never*). Lesson 2 Warnings to children (imperatives with *will* consequences; reflexive pronouns taught with a classroom mirror). Lesson 3 Warnings to parents (imperatives with *Always/Never*; *Why?/Why not?*). Lessons 4–5 Going fishing (Mazin's boat-hire shop at Um Qasr; the first conditional met receptively in the conversation, then studied through its sentence structure — the notes name *may*, *clause* and *result* — and practised in writing). Lesson 6 conditional conversations ("What will we do if we get sick?"). Lesson 7 Danger! (the Amal bathroom conversation; gerunds as nouns — playing with matches, getting lost, drowning; compiling danger lists for home, outside and sea). Lesson 8 Road safety (present tenses and past continuous; describing street pictures; the Layla and Fareed listening). Lesson 9 Round up (with the Nader family car conversation). Lesson 10 Story time "Danger at sea". Unit 7 word list.

**Unit 8 — Revision (pp. 144–154).** Seven lessons: pronunciation of Yes/No questions (intonation; balloon, jet ski, basketball stress); question word order and vocabulary revision; pen-friend advertisements (scanning, Reading tip 2; Rona, Josh, Hans, Nasreen); writing a pen-friend advertisement (tenses, conjunctions); "Keep listening!" — Listening tip 1 (concentrate, predict, don't stop) and the *Our World Today* radio discussion (food, cars, TV/Internet, mobile phones; agreeing and disagreeing), John's story completed with verbs, and conditional sentence writing; "A different kind of holiday" (Reading tips 3 and 4, prediction and inference; the dog on the hillside story); telling and writing a story (the crash helmet/motorbike picture story). Unit 8 word list. There are no Extra activities answers or test papers in this unit's notes beyond the AB53-equivalent revision work of Unit 4's pattern — the unit recycles the whole book for the end-of-year examination.

**Transcript (pp. 155–167) and complete Word list (pp. 168–171).** A consolidated transcript section repeats every audio script in unit order (tracks 1–50), followed by the book's complete alphabetical Word list — every item keyed to unit and lesson ("7.1 = Unit 7, Lesson 1"), non-testable items starred, running from "add 7.1" to "ziggurat* 1.2". Ruled Notes pages (from printed p. 172) and the back cover close the book: the back cover lists the course components (Student's Book, Activity Book, Teacher's Book, Audio), describes the course, repeats the committee box, and is dated 2014.

### Grammar spine (as the Teacher's Book itself maps it, Introduction Section B)

The book's own overview fixes the year's new grammar: adverbs of frequency and *How long/How often* questions (Unit 1) → future with *will* and ordinal dates (Unit 2) → the present perfect for recent actions, with regular and irregular participles (Unit 3) → present perfect for experience with *ever/never*, contrasted with the past simple, and the full comparative/superlative system (Unit 5) → link words and the future continuous (Unit 6) → imperatives with *always/never*, reflexive pronouns, and Type 1 (first) conditionals (Unit 7) — all carried on the assumed base of present/past simple, present and past continuous, possessive *'s*, gerunds after verbs, and the punctuation system, and all recycled in the two review units.
