## Grade 8 — English for Iraq, 2nd Intermediate Student's Book (student's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Student's Book"):** MATCH — the genuine current Garnet student's book for the second intermediate year (Iraqi Grade 8), companion to the 2nd Intermediate Teacher's Book and Activity Book.
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *English for Iraq — 2nd Intermediate Student's Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula; imprint Garnet Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow, Jake Hughes and Paul Drury (four authors; the Teacher's Book names only O'Neill and Snow). Editorial and Adaptation Committee: Malek Mohamed Juda (Head), Adil Ali Moussa, Muayyad Naji Ahmed, Ramzi Nouri Abdullah, Batool Faiyq Abdul-Wahid. Revision Committee: Hind Farooq Ali (Head), Tawadud Mohammed Ridha, Hanaa Adil Ali, Batool Faiyq Abdul-Wahid, Ahmed Saadoon Azeez, Khaldon Wahhab Allawi.
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, printed p. 120):** "First edition © 2015 Garnet Publishing Ltd. Revised edition © 2021 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ21 SB 2ndI".
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — none on the title page or imprint page.
- **PDF pages:** 120, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** zero — Book map is printed pp. 2–3 = PDF pp. 2–3 and the imprint is printed p. 120 = PDF p. 120. PDF p. 1 is the unnumbered title page.
- **Text-layer character:** none — page images only; read whole from 4-up contact sheets.
- **Copy marks:** a small third-party website watermark stamp appears on some pages (a scan stamp, not part of the book), and this is a lightly used copy: a previous owner's handwriting fills in a few exercises (e.g. Jad's application form on p. 11, survey notes on p. 41, a model answer on p. 84). Design: colour, with a recurring serial story, "Jad and Johnny", whose five episodes close Units 1, 2, 3, 5 and 7 (Units 4 and 8 are reviews; Unit 6's story slot is the Alps episode).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Book map (printed pp. 2–3).** Eight units with lexical areas, communicative and language-learning activities, grammatical areas and pronunciation work; four projects (1: My first day at school; 2: Different sports / The sports survey; 3: A chat forum; 4: Recycling at home) and the year-long class school-magazine project.

**Unit 1 — A school magazine (pp. 4–16).** Lesson 1: a girls' school magazine notice (sections: Puzzles, Stories, School news, Town events, Sports reports, Poetry, Cartoons, Jokes, Letters, Problem page, Interviews) with the possessive *'s* box (boy's vs boys'). Lesson 2 A new project: dictionary-style entries teaching (n.)/(v.) labels and word families (article, cover, edit/editor, print/printer, report/reporter), a listening in which boys plan a rival magazine, and the suggestions box (*Why don't we …? / shall / Let's …*). Lessons 3–4 An interview (Parts A and B): "Meet a local personality" — an interview with café owner Jack James that carries present perfect vs past simple with *for/since* ("Talking about time"), *like/love/hate/enjoy/dislike + -ing* ("Talking about likes and dislikes") and describing people with adjectives and defining relative clauses with *who* ("Describing people"). Lesson 5 Problems and Lesson 6 Ask Aunt Sally: a problem page (Jill's shyness letter and Aunt Sally's reply), then two further problems worked through notes and abbreviations (etc., h.w., v., e.g.). Lesson 7 Embarrassing moments: two jumbled first-person stories ("I nearly died!") to reorder. Lesson 8 The Global Youth Magazine: a competition advertisement and Jad Aziz Ali's completed application form. Round up (p. 12), Story time "Jad and Johnny – A tour of London" (pp. 13–14, a Duck Tour on the Thames), Project 1 (pp. 15–16: classroom vocabulary, a subjects crossword, Adam's and Rawan's first-day accounts).

**Unit 2 — Healthy lifestyle (pp. 17–27).** Lesson 1 Keep fit!: the magazine article "Take my advice …" and the advice box *should/shouldn't*. Lesson 2 Eat the right food: food photographs sorted by healthiness, Layla Ahmed's "A report of Nurse Janette's talk", and the quantifier box (*too much, too many, a little, a few, a lot of*). Lesson 3 In a restaurant: a full menu with cooking vocabulary (lamb, beef, fried, grilled, baked) and predicting a restaurant dialogue. Lesson 4 Aches and pains: ailment matching (stomach ache, earache, toothache, headache) and the "Aches" box (*I've got a headache = My head aches*), with four short advice conversations. Lesson 5 Jalal's story: "A true story" by Jalal Abdullah — Jalal ignores his little brother Ibrahim's stomach pain, which proves to be appendicitis; includes the idiom work "You are a pain". Lesson 6 'Get well' cards: matching card fronts to messages. Lesson 7 A letter: Sara's letter from Karrada, Baghdad (dated 4th March 2021) to Muna in hospital. Lesson 8 Limericks: two model limericks and a "Rules for limericks" box (five lines, lines 1/2/5 and 3/4 rhyme, always funny) before writing one. Round up (p. 25) adds time phrases (*once/twice/three times a week*) and *want + object + to*-infinitive. Story time "Jad and Johnny – Camping at Stonehenge" (pp. 26–27).

**Unit 3 — Sports (pp. 28–42).** Lesson 1 The sports centre: the Ashby Sports Centre poster and the *play/go/do* collocations box ("Verbs and sports"), with *would like to*. Lesson 2 The timetable: a recorded message, beginners/advanced/team practice, and *How good are you at …?* Lesson 3 Booking lessons: two telephone dialogues (Liz, Dave) with *learn how to*. Lesson 4 The basketball game: opposites in results (won/lost/beat/lost to; Babil 60, Kirkuk 54) and a conversation completion built on comparatives. Lesson 5 The Olympic Games: "OLYMPIC FACTS AND FIGURES" (ancient Games, Pierre de Coubertin 1896, Rio 2016 figures as printed, medals, the rings). Lesson 6 Olympic superstars: record profiles (Florence Griffith Joyner, Michael Phelps, Inge Sørensen, Oscar Swahn, China's table tennis, Serena Williams, Bob Beamon). Lesson 7 Changes: Saad's planned paragraph "I used to be shy" as a writing model, with the *used to* box ("Talking about changes"). Lesson 8 Have fun with words!: tongue-twisters ("She sells seashells", "Peter Piper pecked a pot of pickled pepper") and how to build one. Round up (p. 36) adds saying decimal numbers and years aloud. Story time "Jad and Johnny – The football match" (pp. 37–38, Bayern Munich's Allianz Arena), Project 2 (pp. 39–42: "Try a new sport!" — skateboarding, Ultimate, surfing; three young people's sports profiles; Umar and Ali's class sports survey with tally, pie and bar charts, then running an own survey).

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 43–46).** Four skills lessons: Lesson 1 Listening (Listening tips 1–3: look and listen for clues; keep listening; write notes with own abbreviations — including listening for the Iraq–Bahrain score). Lesson 2 Reading (Reading tips 2–5: before you read; finding different kinds of information; new words; collecting words in groups). Lesson 3 Speaking (Speaking tips 3–4: reading aloud in word groups; question intonation — voice down after question words, up in other questions; stress marked in practice conversations). Lesson 4 Writing (Writing tips 3–6: check your writing; paint a picture with adjectives and adverbs; make writing interesting; organize and plan each paragraph with topic sentences).

**Unit 5 — Getting and sending information (pp. 47–59).** Lesson 1 Old ways of sending information: photo captions in the past passive and *used to* (the Arab Albarid horse messenger service from 670, lighthouses, flag signals, African drums, smoke signals, carrier pigeons from Egypt to Syria). Lesson 2 Accessing information: a critical-literacy text on encyclopedias, atlases and the internet ("a lot of information does not always mean good information"). Lesson 3 Working out meanings: Reading tip 6 — Dictionaries (alphabetical order, part-of-speech abbreviations, choosing the right meaning of *bank*). Lessons 4–5 From Cairo to Baghdad (Parts A and B): an eight-picture process of a newspaper article's journey, told in present passives, with the "Active and passive" box (*be +* past participle; the agent with *by* usually omitted). Lesson 6 Great inventions: reference-book entries on John Baird (television) and Guglielmo Marconi (radio), with the past active/past passive box. Lesson 7 Telephones: landline vs mobile texts and the box "Present passive with can". Lesson 8 Making telephone calls: Susan Ayad's "SPEAK UP!" advice article and telephone phrase sets. Round up (p. 55), Story time "Jad and Johnny – The gondola trip" (pp. 56–57, Venice; Marcus loses his phone to the canal), Project 3 A chat forum (pp. 58–59: the "Global Chat" thread "Money can buy you happiness", for/against table, and writing a four-paragraph for-and-against essay).

**Unit 6 — Jobs (pp. 60–70).** Lesson 1 What do they do?: seven job descriptions matched to photographs (businesswoman, pilot, architect, mechanic, engineer, shop assistant, firefighter), introducing *a/an* before job names. Lesson 2 Two jobs: parallel texts on Dr Salwa (hospital doctor) and Hanan (office manager) — present simple routines, hours and study histories. Lesson 3 A day in the life of a firefighter: Mazin's day told from a picture sequence with clock times (a supermarket fire, a ring-road crash). Lesson 4 What's my job?: three guessing games with a 21-job word box. Lesson 5 A train timetable: a London–Paris timetable in 24-hour times and station listening. Lesson 6 What are they going to be?: Firas (future zoologist) and Halla (future doctor), the *patient* noun/adjective dictionary entry, and the box "be + going to + infinitive". Lessons 7–8 Career plans (Parts A and B): four self-profiles (Sara, Adel, Khalid, Dania — Dania aims to fly to the Moon) to match to jobs, with the box "Talking about the future with when" (present tense after *when*). Round up (p. 68), Story time "Jad and Johnny – The Alps" (pp. 69–70; Johnny wins a ski race by mistake).

**Unit 7 — The world around us (pp. 71–85).** Lesson 1 World problems: six world newspaper headlines matched to photographs, with the "Headlines" box (headlines are not complete sentences — find the missing verbs and articles). Lesson 2 How can we help?: a charity article ("Send a child to school for 100 dollars!") and a planning dialogue contrasting *should* and *must* ("should and must" box). Lessons 3–4 Animals in danger (Parts A and B): fact files on the snow leopard, ibex, blue whale, golden toad and polar bear; a room furnished with animal products; agreeing/disagreeing phrases; habitats. Lesson 5 Please keep the rivers clean: a four-paragraph text on Iraq's two rivers (pollution by sewage, rubbish and industrial waste; volunteer clean-ups). Lesson 6 A warmer world: a scientist interview on warming, with the "may and might" box. Lesson 7 Throwing things away: wasteful-behaviour pictures and telling people what to do (imperatives with *Don't*). Lesson 8 The moving desert: a newspaper article on desertification in Iraq (donams lost yearly — a donam glossed as 2,500 m²; the drained southern marshes of the 1990s recovering; 'green belt' projects). Round up (p. 79) restores full sentences from headlines ("Sixteen elephants found dead" → "were found dead"). Story time "Jad and Johnny – The Eiffel Tower" (pp. 80–81 — the last episode; the friends part and set up a weekly online group), Project 4 Recycling at home (pp. 82–85: "TURNING TRASH INTO TREASURE!", "THE RUBBISH COMPETITION", describing recycled objects, and illustrated instructions for a plastic-bottle pencil case).

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 86–89).** Four lessons: Lesson 1 Listening (listening carefully for exact information — three people who got details wrong: Susan, Jalal, Jane Smith). Lesson 2 Speaking (reading aloud with feeling; the "Going on holiday" memory game; phoning about job advertisements). Lesson 3 Reading ("A HIGH-FLYING JOB" — Muhammad's road to a pilot's certificate, stressing that English is the 'language of the air'; topic sentences and working out meanings from clues). Lesson 4 Writing (Writing tips 7–11: spelling, punctuation, grammar, joining words, adjectives and adverbs; punctuating a sentence and completing a Ranya/Sulaymaniyah story text with correct verb forms).

**Transcripts (pp. 90–109) and Grammar and functions reference (pp. 110–119).** Full transcripts including the Extra activities listenings. The reference gives form/use tables per unit: possessive *'s* incl. irregular plurals, *for/since*, *like* + noun/-ing/adjective + noun (Unit 1); *should/shouldn't*, quantifiers, *want* patterns (Unit 2); *do/go/play*, descriptions (adjective vs adverb), *used to* (Unit 3); active and passive forms, a verb table, past active/past passive, present passive with *can* (Unit 5); *be going to*, *be good at*, future with *be going to* and *when* (Unit 6); *should and must*, *may and might* (Unit 7). Imprint and photography credits close the book (printed p. 120).

### Grammar spine

Possessive *'s* incl. plural and irregular-plural apostrophes (Unit 1) → *like/enjoy/hate/love/dislike + -ing* (Unit 1) → defining relative clauses with *who* for describing people (Unit 1) → present perfect vs past simple with *for/since* (Unit 1) → *should/shouldn't* for advice (Unit 2) → quantifiers *too much/too many/a little/a few/a lot of* (Unit 2) → *have/have got* for ailments (Unit 2) → *want + object + to*-infinitive (Unit 2) → *play/go/do* with sports, *would like to*, *How good are you at …?*, *learn how to* (Unit 3) → comparatives in conversation (Unit 3) → *used to* for past states and changes (Units 3 and 5) → present and past simple passive and passive with *can* (Unit 5) → the indefinite article before job names (Unit 6) → *be going to* future, including *When + present simple, … going to …* (Unit 6) → *should* vs *must* (Unit 7) → *may/might* and *It's possible that …* for possibility (Unit 7) → headline grammar (missing verbs and articles) (Unit 7).

### Honest negatives

- **No present perfect continuous, no past continuous as a new point (it is G7 revision only), no second or third conditionals, no reported speech in the Student's Book** (the first conditional appears only as Unit 7 revision), and the passive is limited to present/past simple and *can +* passive. **No end-of-book word list** — unlike the Teacher's Book, the Student's Book closes with transcripts and the grammar reference only.
- The Grammar and functions reference has **no entries for Units 4 and 8** (review units) — as printed.
- **A misprint in the reference (printed p. 116):** in the "Past active" section, questions are said to be formed with "was/were + subject + past simple" — the wrong auxiliary for the active past as printed (the passive examples below it are correct). Recorded as printed.
- Content dates itself in places (Rio 2016 Olympic figures; a letter dated 4th March 2021); recorded as printed.
- Scan: complete and in order, 120/120 pages; the handwriting and watermark marks noted above are the only copy defects.

## Grade 8 — English for Iraq, 2nd Intermediate Activity Book (activity book / workbook)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Activity Book"):** MATCH — the genuine companion workbook to the 2nd Intermediate Student's Book.
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *English for Iraq — 2nd Intermediate Activity Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula; imprint Garnet Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow and Paul Drury (three names — the Student's Book adds Jake Hughes; the Teacher's Book names only O'Neill and Snow).
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, printed p. 2):** "First edition © 2015 Garnet Publishing Ltd. Revised edition © 2021 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ21 AB 2ndI".
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — none on the title page or imprint page.
- **PDF pages:** 127, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** zero — the imprint is printed p. 2 = PDF p. 2, "This book belongs to" (with a photograph of the Gilgamesh statue) is printed p. 3 = PDF p. 3, and the last page is printed p. 127 = PDF p. 127. PDF p. 1 is the unnumbered title page.
- **Text-layer character:** none — page images only; read whole from 4-up contact sheets. Printed in black and white.
- **Copy marks:** the same small third-party website watermark stamp as the other band copies appears on some pages; a scan stamp, not part of the book.

### Unit-by-unit summary

The workbook mirrors the Student's Book lesson by lesson, reusing its texts for written work and adding its own boxes, tips, puzzles and several workbook-only texts.

**Unit 1 — A school magazine (printed pp. 4–21).** Magazine-section vocabulary and matching extracts to sections; the box "Possessive 's'" (regular vs irregular plurals) with gap-fills; listening answers and syllable-stress marking (magazine, reporter, interview, photographer, photograph); writing job definitions with relative clauses ("somebody who …") for -er jobs; writing an own suggestions conversation; a second, workbook-only "Meet a local personality" interview — headteacher Yahya Zeki — with comprehension questions, personal interview answers, *How long have you …?* question writing, *for/since* completion and adjective-to-relative-clause rewriting; problem-page vocabulary (stammer, confident, frightened), judging and improving advice, and the box "very, very for emphasis"; the box "Punctuation – Speech marks" with punctuating direct speech and writing an advice conversation; Aunt Sally's notes turned into advice paragraphs, an abbreviations exercise (a.m., cm, hr, km, kph, m, min., mm, p.m., sec.); Reading tip 1 – Pronouns and there, used to order the embarrassing-moments stories; idiom work ("I nearly died!", black eye); Writing tip 1 – A paragraph, an Opinions box (asking for and giving opinions), writing an own embarrassing-moment story; the workbook prints Johnny Miller's and Paolo Guidi's Global Youth Magazine application forms for comparison with Jad's, plus a blank form to complete; a Round up rewriting likes/dislikes as relative clauses, punctuating a paragraph, and expanding note-style messages (Pls., v., h.w.) into full sentences; Story time story-notes and questions on the London tour.

**Unit 2 — Healthy lifestyle (pp. 22–39).** Paragraph-topic matching and questions on the fitness article; rewriting imperatives as *should/shouldn't* advice; the box "Answering How often questions"; self-advice writing; a food spider diagram (very good / not so good for you) and report questions; the quantifier box (*too much, too many, a little, a few, a lot of* — with countable/uncountable rules to complete) and sentence completion; the restaurant listening marked on the menu (M/W/F), ordering phrases, role-play orders and cooking-word definitions; ailment conversation completion and the two doctor listening note-sets (Noora's ear drops; Rasha's glasses and painkillers) turned into paragraphs; feelings in situations; *want + object + to*-infinitive sentence building from a table; correcting Jalal's story draft using teacher's correction marks (join, gr., sp., n.p., P.); making a 'Get well' card to a measured size, with acrostic GET WELL, funny and serious message models; correcting the 11 marked mistakes in Sara's draft letter (spelling, wrong word, tense, singular) and planning an own letter; ordering and completing jumbled limericks (the spider and the chicken, the Kuwait schoolboy, the man from Jaipur); a Round up of word order, speech punctuation and feelings adjectives; Story time notes on Stonehenge.

**Unit 3 — Sports (pp. 40–57).** Sports matched from word clues and sorted into *do/play/go* columns; spelling correction (sentre, biginers, afeletics, teem); photo-based timetable questions; filling the Ashby Sports Centre timetable from two listenings and the leaflet; *do/go/play* in all tenses in sentence completion; sports-officer role-play frames; Hilal and Samir's conversation completed with comparative adjectives and adverbs (better, faster, fitter, harder, lazier, more slowly, more unfit, worse) beside the box "Descriptions" (adjective vs adverb); Speaking tip 1 – Measurements (saying decimals: "twelve point five seconds") and Speaking tip 2 – Years (1981 "nineteen eighty-one", 2022 "twenty twenty-two"); a picture-story told and written (Jane and Mary's race, high jump and sea rescue); correcting false statements about the Olympic article; a fastest-finger quiz on the superstars text; athlete profiles modelled on Cathy Freeman, including choosing a famous Iraqi athlete; *used to* sentence-making about Saad and an own changes table; Writing tip 2 – Topic sentences, with a planned paragraph (topic sentence, note diagram, order, draft, correct); building tongue-twisters from sound groups; a Round up verb table (present/past/past participle) and a sports word-tree puzzle whose clues include "Kirkuk 2, Babil 1"; Story time notes and true/false on the football match.

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 58–61).** Decoding abbreviated listening notes (Mr and Mrs Andrews' orders); the Wendy listening with multiple-choice answers; a workbook-only reading text, "Cleaner streets" — the three Salih brothers' Green Team removing ten tonnes of rubbish in 365 days — with questions; building word groups for reading aloud (back-chaining from "volleyball" to "I'd like to play volleyball"), separating sentences into groups, and writing questions for given answers; improving a deliberately faulty postcard (Muhannad's) using the Student's Book writing tips and rewriting it in best handwriting. **Extra activities for Units 1–4 follow at pp. 62–65:** speech-mark punctuation, the magazine-sections listening and a verb-form story completion (Unit 1); a workbook-only story of Salam and his little brother Kamal's fall from a tree, with questions (Unit 2); the Sports Weekend timetable listening and a Babil–Kirkuk match paragraph completion (Unit 3); an 80-word paragraph task using *used to*, planned on a topic-sentence diagram (Unit 4).

**Unit 5 — Getting and sending information (pp. 66–83).** Old-ways vocabulary completion and *used to … but now* sentence writing; a three-paragraph planned essay on getting and sending information with given topic sentences; information vocabulary (access, find out, looked it up, references, sources) and the three-people listening table; a workbook-only story, Nabeel and the computer that didn't work, with questions; sorting words into conjunction/preposition/pronoun columns and labelling every word in sentences with part-of-speech abbreviations; Reading tip 7 – Working out meanings (the "chesterfield" worked example) and synonym multiple choice (depressed, speechless, cautious, apologetic); the Cairo-to-Baghdad process completed in the present passive; the box "Active forms" — rewriting "Life in Iraq" passives as actives, and writing own pairs; a verb-form table (infinitive/past/past participle) and describing how fish get from the sea to the shops in present passives; invention true/false and active↔passive rewriting (Marconi, Baird); ordering inventions in time (canned food 1810, washing machine 1901, sticky notes 1980, smartphone 2007, as keyed by the exercise) and past passive completions; completing a reported message, T/F/DS statements on the phone texts, matching app advertisements to what the apps do in passive sentences ("Your steps can be counted"), and writing about a home-control app; notes on Anas's good phone call and writing a receptionist's questions and answers; back-to-back telephone role-plays; a Round up of spelling gaps, definitions, text-message ordering and passive completions ("Assur was built over 4,000 years ago"); Story time notes on the gondola trip.

**Unit 6 — Jobs (pp. 84–101).** Matching job descriptions to photographs and building nine job names from halves (businesswoman, engineer, architect, pilot, mechanic, dentist, firefighter, teacher, doctor); job questions and "whose help do they need?" scenarios; note tables comparing Dr Salwa and Hanan, turned into questions; writing questions for nurse Nadia's answers (the Red Crescent Hospital listening); ordering the 14 jumbled sentences of firefighter Mazin's day, vocabulary multiple choice (equipment, trapped, upside down, terrible, injured, put the fire out) and opinion questions; matching job descriptions, job-word connections and odd-one-out sets; grouping guessing-game questions under three jobs; the train-passenger listening with tick-box answers; completing polite ticket-office expressions, a "Conversation" politeness box, and station role-plays; short answers on Firas and Halla; take/pass/fail exam sentences; *going to* matching and personal predictions; completing Sara's and Adel's career-plan texts; the box "be + good at/not good at + noun or -ing" with error correction; identifying who says each career statement (vet, carpenter, translator, astronaut) and personality adjectives from behaviour descriptions; writing and reading aloud an own career plan; a Round up of job spelling, subject connections and question writing; Story time notes on The Alps.

**Unit 7 — The world around us (pp. 102–119).** Matching headlines to photographs and rewriting full sentences as headlines ("The moving desert is swallowing up Iraq"); syllable stress, *should/must* completion, bring-and-buy listening notes and a conversation frame with it/them choices; a letter about a bring-and-buy sale; a workbook-only newspaper report, "The big clean-up" (Ahmed Ayoob, schoolgirl Jameela, Saeed Abdullah), with headline choice and questions; animals recall gap-fill, agreeing/disagreeing conversation completion and true/false; habitat matching (llama, Arctic fox, orca, pelican, otter, camel, seal, goat, fennec fox) and description-to-animal matching; agree/disagree with reasons on environmental statements; notes and a paragraph on an Iraqi animal in danger; rivers text questions and a workbook-only magazine article, "THE RIVERBANK NEEDS OUR HELP!", with meeting follow-ups and drafting an own clean-up article; warmer-world questions and *may/might* word-order and completion exercises, including hedged climate-action sentences ("I might travel less, but …"); the "Waste not, want not!" honesty questionnaire; moving-desert vocabulary definitions and short answers; three workbook-only newspaper articles (Ethiopia's 2019 tree-planting campaign, Jadav Payeng's forest island, tree-planting advice) with questions; a Round up (missing letters, vocabulary completion, headlines expanded to full sentences — including restoring the passive in "Two killed in hotel fire", *should/must*, agree/disagree); Story time notes and true/false on the Eiffel Tower.

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 120–123).** The radio fire news report (best headline; short answers) and the Kamal Mustafa teacher listening; Word chains and an animal guessing game with Yes/No questions, plus a job word-tree; a workbook-only reading article, "My hobby" — a fish-keeper whose favourite fish is the great white shark — with paragraph topics and questions; the Ranya/Sulaymaniyah verb-form text, punctuation, and writing an ~80-word paragraph, "The best day of my life". **Extra activities for Units 5–8 close the book at pp. 124–127:** Noora and Zeena true/false and writing Noora's email to her German penfriend Helga (Unit 5); the future-jobs listening and career vocabulary (Unit 6); the beach-litter listening and definition writing (Unit 7); mixed verb-form completions, *should or must*, and rewriting actives as passives ("Lots of cars are made in Japan") (Unit 8). There is no final Notes page in this book — p. 127 is the last Extra activities page.

### Grammar spine (as practised and boxed in this workbook)

Possessive *'s* with regular and irregular plurals (Unit 1) → present perfect with *for/since* and *How long have you …?* (Unit 1) → relative clauses with *who* after *like* (Unit 1) → *very, very* emphasis (Unit 1) → speech marks for direct speech (Unit 1) → *should/shouldn't* (Unit 2) → quantifiers with countable/uncountable rules (Unit 2) → *want + object + to*-infinitive (Unit 2) → *do/go/play* across tenses (Unit 3) → comparatives of adjectives and adverbs (Unit 3) → *used to* (Units 3–4) → parts of speech and dictionary abbreviations (Unit 5) → present passive, past passive and active↔passive conversion (Unit 5) → polite question forms (Unit 6) → *be going to* and *be good at + -ing* (Unit 6) → *should vs must* (Unit 7) → *may/might* for possibility, including in first-conditional sentences (Unit 7) → headline grammar both ways (Units 7–8).

### Honest negatives

- As with the Grade 7 workbook: **no answer key and no transcripts**; listening exercises depend on the course audio. Almost all grammar is the Student's Book's, re-practised; the workbook's own additions are its boxes and tips (speech marks, pronouns and *there*, measurements and years pronunciation, topic sentences, working out meanings, active forms) and its extra reading texts, which are listed in the unit summaries because they are content a filing would otherwise miss.
- **No word list** and no closing Notes page (the Grade 7 Activity Book has one; this book ends with Extra activities).
- Scan: complete and in order, 127/127 pages, greyscale, legible throughout; only the small third-party watermark stamps noted above.

## Grade 8 — English for Iraq, 2nd Intermediate Teacher's Book (teacher's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Teacher's Guide"):** MATCH in substance — this is the genuine Garnet teacher's book for the second intermediate year (Iraqi Grade 8). The book's own name for itself is "Teacher's Book", not "Teacher's Guide".
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *2nd Intermediate Teacher's Book*, in the series *English for Iraq*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill and Peter Snow; Editorial and Adaptation Committee — Ministry of Education English Curriculum Committee: Malek Mohamed Juda (Head of the English Curriculum), Muayyad Naji Ahmed (Curriculum Consultant), Batool Faeq Abdulwahid (Senior Education Specialist).
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK (the text layer's OCR renders the postcode imperfectly; the publisher and address are as printed).
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, PDF p. 2):** "First edition, copyright © 2015 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug printed on the imprint page: "IRQ15 TB 2ndI".
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — no ISBN string occurs anywhere in the book's complete text layer; the imprint carries the production slug instead.
- **PDF pages:** 172.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** the book prints its own folios in the footer ("<folio> English for Iraq Teacher's Book 2nd Intermediate: <section>"), and the last page is printed p. 172 = PDF p. 172. The copy was photographed in two-page spreads (slivers of facing pages are visible at some edges) and a previous owner hand-renumbered some pages, so mid-book the scan drifts slightly against the printed folio (spot checks: PDF p. 51 shows the printed folio 49; PDF p. 101 shows printed folio 100). Anchor page numbers below are therefore given as PDF pages, which is what a checker can verify against this file; the book's internal cross-references are to Student's Book and Activity Book pages (SB/AB numbers).
- **Text-layer character:** full embedded OCR text layer from the scanner software — noisy (systematic misreads such as "Irog" for Iraq, "Carnet" for Garnet) but substantively complete on all 172 pages; read whole from the text layer, with the title and imprint pages also checked against the printed wording.
- **Cast/design:** the Student's Book world it teaches around — school-magazine classes at a girls' school and a boys' school, and the serial story characters Bob and Basim.

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Introduction — Sections A–D (front of the book).** Section A describes the course components (Student's Book, Activity Book, audio CD), the symbols and Round up pages, the Teacher's Book's own layout (unit content summaries, transcripts, a complete word list per unit, lesson-plan tables giving materials, objectives, language and new/non-testable vocabulary) and the organisation of the year: eight units, 74 lessons in total, core Units 1–3 and 5–7 of ten lessons each, revision Units 4 and 8 of eight lessons each, Lesson 10 of each core unit given to extensive reading ("Story time") and marking the unit test. A key feature is the year-long school-magazine project, with project sections after Units 2, 5 and 7. Section B is an overview of the main grammatical areas for the whole intermediate stage (items marked with the unit of first occurrence). Sections C–D give classroom advice and teaching techniques: pair/group work, using Arabic sparingly, checking understanding, reading-aloud types, dealing with unfamiliar vocabulary, quizzes, skimming, the writing process through to a final draft, dictation procedure (TP D1) and running a class editorial board.

**Unit 1 — A school magazine (SB pp. 4–15).** The year's project is launched: a girls' school class magazine and a boys' school group planning their own magazine; pupils read a magazine interview, a problem page and a short story page, then plan their own magazine's contents and management. Language: the apostrophe before and after *s* and speech marks; *Why don't we …? / Let's …* and *will/shall* for making plans; *like, enjoy, hate, love, dislike +* gerund; defining relative clauses with *who*; present perfect and past simple with *for/since*; imperatives positive and negative in the problem-page advice; the serial story Bob and Basim begins.

**Unit 2 — Healthy lifestyle (SB pp. 17–27).** Keeping fit, healthy eating, ordering a meal in a restaurant and describing aches and pains; pupils make Get well cards, read the narrative "Jalal's story" and study two teacher-marked paragraphs, learning to correct marked writing. Language: *should/shouldn't +* infinitive for advice; adverbial time phrases *once/twice/three times a day/week*; quantifiers *a little, a few, a lot, too much, too many*; *have/have got* and *My (head) aches* for health; the pattern *want + noun/pronoun + to +* infinitive; new line and paragraphing in writing. Project section 1 (school magazine) follows the unit.

**Unit 3 — Sports (SB pp. 32–41).** Sports preferences and a sports centre: reading a leaflet and timetable, booking classes, a basketball-game conversation and narrating a story from picture cues. Language: *What sports would you like to play/do? — I'd like to …*; *How good are you at …?*; *learn how to +* infinitive; past habitual *used to +* infinitive; comparatives; decimal numbers and measurements read aloud (e.g. "twelve point five seconds", "one point four five metres"); the apostrophe in irregular plural nouns (*men's*).

**Unit 4 — Review (SB pp. 43–46; AB tests).** Four Student's Book lessons reviewing the advice given earlier in the course on improving listening, speaking, reading and writing skills, plus a four-lesson test in the Activity Book. No new vocabulary; the introduction states the unit can be omitted if a class has fallen behind.

**Unit 5 — Getting and sending information (SB pp. 47–61).** Old and modern ways of sending information, accessing information, dictionary skills (parts of speech, reading a dictionary entry, working out meaning from context), the illustrated process text "From Cairo to Baghdad", great inventions, telephones and advice on making telephone calls. Language: the present and past simple passive; modal *can +* present passive; *used to +* infinitive for old methods; imperatives with *do* for emphasis in advice. Project section 2 is themed on the date palm (history of the date palm in Iraq, madgooga recipes for the magazine).

**Unit 6 — Jobs (SB pp. 62–71).** Names of jobs and school subjects, two jobs read in detail, a day in the life of a firefighter told from pictures, the guessing game "What's my job?", and a comparison of an English school timetable with the pupils' own, including syllable stress. Language: the article before names of jobs (*He's/She's a/an …*); revision of present simple statements and questions and of the past simple; the *going to* future, including the pattern *When + present tense, I'm going to …*; the past passive appears in the timetable lesson's language focus.

**Unit 7 — The world around us (SB pp. 72–83).** Six newspaper headlines open a deliberately more adult unit: world problems, a charity appeal ("How can we help?"), endangered animals, keeping rivers clean, and a warmer world, with the project "Trash or treasure?" (inventing a way of reusing plastic bottles). Language: the form of headlines; modals *must, should* and *will* in offers; agreeing and disagreeing (*I agree/disagree. I think you're right/wrong*); modals *may, might* and *It's possible that …* for possibility; revision of *going to*, the first conditional and the past simple; giving orders to litter-droppers. The unit ends with the Student's Book Round up (SB p. 81) and Bob and Basim — the medals (SB pp. 82–83).

**Unit 8 — Review (SB pp. 88–91; AB pp. 109–114).** The same shape as Unit 4: lessons reviewing listening (predicting situations from pictures and text), speaking (reading aloud, a memorisation game, telephone conversations), reading (scanning for paragraph topics, working out meanings) and writing, followed by Tests A (listening, grammar and vocabulary), B (reading and answering questions) and C (writing a paragraph of about 80 words, e.g. "The best day of my life"), and a final lesson returning the tests and evaluating performance.

**Word list (end of the book).** A complete unit-by-unit word list for the year, marking testable items, with the notation "(n) = noun, (v) = verb, (adj) = adjective, (adv) = adverb, (prep) = preposition" and "(Gr)" for grammar terms such as *adjective, noun, pronoun, relative clause*.

### Grammar spine

New in this year, per the unit overviews and the Section B grammar overview: defining relative clauses with *who* (Unit 1); present perfect vs past simple with *for/since* (Unit 1, revision); *like/enjoy/hate/love/dislike +* gerund (Unit 1); quantifiers *a little/a few/too much/too many/a lot* and *want + object + to*-infinitive (Unit 2); *would like to*, *How good are you at …?*, *learn how to* and past-habitual *used to* (Unit 3); the present and past simple passive and *can +* passive (Unit 5); the *going to* future with *When + present, I'm going to …* (Unit 6); modals *must/may/might* and revision of the first conditional (Unit 7). Everything else in the Section B overview — comparatives and superlatives, adverbs of frequency and manner, adverbial clauses of reason and time, prepositions, possessives — is listed as introduced in earlier grades and revised here.

### Citation-check section

Verified as a separate pass: each anchor below was checked against that single page of the real PDF. Method: the book's embedded scanner OCR text layer was searched page by page (14 anchors); the 6 anchors the noisy layer garbles (title/imprint display type, "Irog"/"Carnet"-type misreads) were confirmed visually on the rendered page. Anchor page numbers are PDF pages, as given in the draft — this scan drifts against the printed folios mid-book (e.g. PDF p. 38 carries printed folio 36). 20 anchors, all passed.

### Honest negatives

- **The Teacher's Book teaches no new grammar beyond its Student's Book** — it is a teaching apparatus (procedures, transcripts, answers, tests and word lists), not a parallel course; two of its eight units (4 and 8) are explicitly review-and-test only, with no new vocabulary.
- **No present perfect continuous, no past continuous as a new point (it appears only inside a Section B example sentence), no second/third conditional teaching (first conditional is revision only), no reported speech, no passive beyond the simple present/past forms and *can +* passive.**
- **Copy defects:** the scan's OCR text layer is heavily corrupted at the character level (proper names and display type suffer most — the title page's "Iraq", "Garnet" and the authors' names are all misread in the layer), some introduction pages are two-column interleaved, and a small number of pages are largely garbled display type (notably the Section A feature page). The underlying printed book is complete: all 172 PDF pages carry substantive content and the unit sequence 1–8 plus word list is intact.
- The book assumes the audio CD throughout ("CD" references in every unit table); no audio is part of this file.

## Grade 9 — English for Iraq, 3rd Intermediate Student's Book (student's book)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Student's Book"):** MATCH — the genuine current Garnet student's book for the third intermediate year (Iraqi Grade 9).
- **Title as printed (title page, PDF p. 1):** *English for Iraq — 3rd Intermediate Student's Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education, General Directorate of Curricula; imprint Garnet Education.
- **Authors as printed:** Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow and Sally Etherton. (Committee boxes on the title page are printed in small type; the Editorial and Adaptation Committee and Revision Committee continue from the earlier books of the series.)
- **Publisher:** Garnet Publishing Ltd., 8 Southern Court, South Street, Reading RG1 4QS, UK.
- **Edition/year as printed (imprint, printed p. 120):** "First edition © 2015 Garnet Publishing Ltd. Revised edition © 2023 Garnet Publishing Ltd." Production slug: "IRQ23 SB 3rdI". This is the newest revision in the band (the Grade 7/8 books are the 2021 revision).
- **ISBN as printed:** no ISBN printed — none on the title page or imprint page.
- **PDF pages:** 120, all read.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** zero — Book map is printed pp. 2–3 = PDF pp. 2–3 and the imprint is printed p. 120 = PDF p. 120. PDF p. 1 is the unnumbered title page.
- **Text-layer character:** partial — a real text layer exists only on PDF pp. 4–17 (Unit 1); the remaining pages are images only. The whole book was read from 4-up contact sheets, with the Unit 1 text layer used as a cross-check.
- **Design/cast:** colour; unlike Grade 8 there is no single serial story — each unit has its own standalone Story time text. Recurring contexts include Al-Quds Intermediate School, the *Iraqi Morning News* and Al Sabah newspaper, and prices in Iraqi dinars (IQD).

### Unit-by-unit summary

**Book map (printed pp. 2–3).** Eight units: 1 Describing things; 2 Spare time activities; 3 Our world; 4 Review; 5 The media; 6 Jobs and careers; 7 Other countries; 8 Review, with four projects (1: More than just shopping!; 2: Helping wildlife!; 3: People who make a difference; 4: Perform a story).

**Unit 1 — Describing things (pp. 4–17).** Lesson 1 At the mall: a Manchester mall photograph with mall vocabulary (cinema, clock, escalator, information desk, lift, plant, queue, security guard, shop assistant), Richard's description of the mall, and the "Describing places" box (*There is/are …, It has …, It doesn't have …*). Lesson 2 Hurry up! It's time to go!: matching analogue and digital clocks and saying the time, then four phone conversations at the information desk built on the present continuous for now and the past continuous for earlier ("What were you doing?"). Lesson 3 Car of the Year!: car features (airbags, Bluetooth connection, electric windows, GPS, sunroof, tinted windows) and a newspaper article, "CAR OF THE YEAR AT ONE OF BAGHDAD'S MOTOR SHOWS!", on the hybrid Panther 3.0D. Lesson 4 Buying and selling: describing a mountain bike and a lightweight tent, with the boxes "Compound words" (windproof, disc brakes, insect-proof) and "Giving reasons" (*to*-infinitive and *so* clauses). Lesson 5 What are they for?: four adverts (phone covers, sunglasses, trainers, skateboards) matched to buyers' comments — adjective-rich description. Lesson 6 What do they look like?: describing Sally and Ahmed (is wearing / has / is quite/a bit) and character adjectives (artistic, friendly, hard-working, musical, sporty). Lesson 7 Good friends: two emails (Dina on Sally; Zaid on Ahmed) mixing appearance and character with modifiers *very, quite, really*. Lesson 8 Writing an advert: comparing three smartwatch adverts (MaxOut 500 at 1,300,000 IQD; Slimline 40; The Watcher 2.0) with superlatives and choosing one with reasons. Round up (p. 13) adds the adjective order table (Opinion–Size–Age–Shape–Colour–Origin–Material). Story time "Lucky customer 50" (pp. 14–15): Kareem queues at Al Saqi Bookshop on Al Mutanabbi Street for poet Mudhafar Al-Nawab's *Islands of Salt* and, as the 50th customer, wins a meeting with the poet. Project 1 (pp. 16–17): comparing a StudyMall and an EcoMall from a mall floor-plan, then designing a new kind of mall.

**Unit 2 — Spare time activities (pp. 18–29).** Lesson 1 A perfect holiday: a "Tourist City" advertisement (swimming, sailing, fishing, camping, museum, picnic, campfire), true/false checking, Reema's listening, and the "Talking about likes and dislikes" box (love/enjoy/like/don't like/hate + -ing). Lesson 2 Spare time: ordering a dialogue and the "Expressing preferences and making suggestions" table (*I'd like/I'd love/Would you like + to*; *I'd rather/Why not/Let's +* bare infinitive; *How about + -ing*). Lesson 3 My favourite kind of book: matching covers and reviews to genres (adventure, comedy, fiction, horror, travel) — Destination Egypt, The Black Cat, Robinson Crusoe (with a short retelling), The Fellowship of the Ring, Mr Bean. Lesson 4 Work or play?: matching interview questions and answers for TV comedy star Lucy Fisher, with the "Asking about the present and the past" box. Lesson 5 Two conversations: parallel film conversations (a horror film, The Night of the Killer Cockroaches; a factual film, Taxi City) as a discussion frame. Lesson 6 A TV comedy: a STAR! Magazine article, "Schoolgirl in new TV comedy", about Lucy Fisher's role as Samara in *The Badri Family*, with sentences matched to episode photographs. Lesson 7 Invitations: adverts and emails for a school graduation party and the Happy Town Circus in Al-Jadiriyah (seats 10,000–30,000 IQD), and an invitation dialogue to role-play. Lesson 8 My favourite movie: a complete Fact File on *The Greatest Showman* (2017) — credits, story summary of PT Barnum, interesting facts and the writer's opinion. Round up (p. 27) systematises present and past simple question forms. Story time "The champion" (pp. 28–29): basketball player Karam loses his foot saving a little girl from a car and returns as a wheelchair basketball international.

**Unit 3 — Our world (pp. 30–43).** Lesson 1 Our world: animal and habitat categories (wild animals, birds, farm animals, habitats) described from photographs (bear, camel, jackal, duck, sheep, stork), with the "Comparing things with adjectives" box (comparative formation, *more/less*, and *(not) as … as*). Lesson 2 Our growing world: headlines "People Growing Taller", "People Living Longer", "Population Increasing" matched to data phrases, and a discussion in first conditionals ("If the population increases, we will need more food"). Lesson 3 I hate spiders: expressing attitudes (I hate / I don't mind / I'm afraid of / So do I / Neither do I) and a listening about Kareem and Rami and a spider. Lesson 4 If we didn't have any spiders …: the "Talking about imaginary situations" box — the second conditional (*If +* past simple, *would +* infinitive) — practised from sentence beginnings and endings. Lesson 5 The fastest living things: a text on falconry (falcons diving at 240 kph as printed; the Bedouin, the houbara, modern Iraqi falconers and protected areas). Lesson 6 Do you care about wildlife?: a scored questionnaire, "CARING FOR THE NATURAL WORLD". Lesson 7 Some of Iraq's animals: listening descriptions matched to photographs (hedgehog, bat, snake, gazelle, ibex, wolf). Lesson 8 Life on the marshes: a Fact File on the Marsh Arabs (reed houses, mashhoof and tarrada boats, forty bird species, the 1990s draining and partial restoration) with an "Active and passive" noticing box (*can be found*). Round up (p. 39) includes the full pronoun table (subject/object/possessive adjective) and *used to* for past comparison. Story time "How to fill a space" (pp. 40–41): the folk tale of the merchant's three sons — Majid fills the room with straw, Ali with feathers, and Hamad with the light of a candle. Project 2 (pp. 42–43): "Helping our bees" (making a bee helper for solitary bees) and four bird-feeder instructions to match and build.

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 44–49).** Lesson 1 Listening: Listening tips 1–3 (listen for the loud, slow words; listen for the loud sounds in long words — stress; listen for repetition), applied to announcements including a missing-boy report. Lesson 2 Reading: Reading tips 1–4 (look at pictures/photographs; look at the headline or title; read the first paragraph; read the topic sentences), applied to earlier texts in the book. Lesson 3 Speaking: Speaking tips 1–4 (ask people to repeat information; repeat information; react to opinions; ask open questions) with closed-vs-open question contrasts. Lesson 4 Writing: Writing tips 1–4 (make a plan; write a summary with topic sentences; make notes of the information in each paragraph; write sentences from your notes), worked through a summary of *Gulliver's Travels*.

**Unit 5 — The media (pp. 50–63).** Lesson 1 Watching the match: sports equipment matching and talking about watching sport. Lesson 2 Nawal Ramzi talks to the *Iraqi Morning News*: an interview with the Mosul-born dentist, carrying the "Narrating a story" box (past continuous background events vs past simple story events). Lessons 3–4 Read all about it! / Where can I find it?: a full mock newspaper page dated 21 June 2016 ("Minister of Education in London", "Minister at opening ceremony", "Boy saves child", small ads) and a sections index (Home News, International News, Arts, Sport, Business, Television, Cartoons) with extracts to file. Lesson 5 Graduation party reviews: Dalia's and Sara's contrasting reviews of the Unit 2 party, with a "Remember" box contrasting the two past tenses and converting past continuous clauses to past simple. Lesson 6 Would you take less?: two "BIKES FOR SALE" adverts and a ten-question bargaining dialogue (a mountain bike at 75,000 IQD — "Well, maybe 70,000"; a town bike at 50,000 IQD — "No, sorry, 50,000 is a good price"). Lesson 7 When will the School activity week start?: a timetable of events and the "Remember" box on future *will* for uncontrollable/scheduled events. Lesson 8 A website for me: reading popularity data, running a class survey with tally charts, and reporting with *most, least, some and a few*. Round up (p. 59) adds subject/object pronouns and *the* before certain country names (the UK, the UAE). Story time "A powerful lesson for everyone" (pp. 60–61): schoolboy reporter Salam Ahmed's prize story — a son quietly cares for his old father in a restaurant, teaching onlookers a lesson in respect. Project 3 (pp. 62–63): from interview notes ("Interview with Aunt Sana") to Adil Alsarraf's magazine article "Inspiring Nurse Makes a Difference", then interviewing an own person who makes a difference.

**Unit 6 — Jobs and careers (pp. 64–75).** Lesson 1 Jobs and workplaces: naming workplaces from photographs, the definition pattern "A mechanic is someone who works in a garage …", "Someone who …" definitions for ten jobs, and a Who am I? guessing game. Lesson 2 Careers advice: the "Talking about the future" box (*be going to* for plans vs *will* for certainties) inside two headteacher interviews — twins Layla (no plan; leaving at 16) and Dania (aiming to be a vet). Lesson 3 What should I do?: Mona's advice blog with four replies of varying sense (Fatima, Fouza, Leena, Dalia) to judge and answer. Lesson 4 The school of the future: Hassan's competition entry (virtual learning, one 'super school' per town, global education) with the relative-pronouns box "Giving more information about people and things" (*who* for people, *which/that* for things). Lesson 5 A, an or the?: the full articles box (a/an general and before jobs; the for particular and unique things; zero article before subjects, place names and after *like*) practised inside two conversations. Lesson 6 How do you get to …?: asking for and giving directions on a Baghdad street map (Al-Rasheed Street, Al-Mutanabbi Street bookshop). Lesson 7 A famous career: a biography of Ibn al-Haitham of Basra (born 965; the *Book of Optics* in seven volumes; UNESCO's 2015 celebration), with an eye-diagram plate. Lesson 8 A school project: Faisal's interview notes on Bob Pearson, a British teacher working for Iraq Petroleum, from which students reconstruct the questions. Round up (p. 73), Story time "Hard work pays" (pp. 74–75): Dalia, orphaned and poor, studies hard, transforms a farm with modern management and ends running her own company while her idle classmates look on.

**Unit 7 — Other countries (pp. 76–89).** Lesson 1 Other countries: inferring holiday destinations from souvenirs (Turkey, France, Japan, Lebanon, London, Mecca, Egypt, India). Lesson 2 Different countries, different customs: scanning two short texts (transport customs; British business hours) and the "Time adverbs" box (definite vs indefinite). Lesson 3 Travel adventures: "TV this week" — three travel programmes (John Wilson down the Nile; Alan Bridges' 1,000-km horseback journey through the Himalayas from India to China; Iraqi Fareed Lafta's balloon crossing of the Sahara, ending in a helicopter rescue in Niger). Lesson 4 How? Where? When?: the "Adverbs and adverbials" and "Word order" boxes (indefinite time/frequency adverbs before the verb; definite time at the edges; manner and place after the verb/object; Manner–Place–Time order). Lesson 5 I'm sorry …: the "Apologizing" box (making and responding to polite apologies; *Pardon?*) with role-plays. Lesson 6 How many are made each day?: matching statistics sentences in present and past passives to three headlines (Kia car plants, Indian phone production at 290 million, Iraqi oil output doubling). Lesson 7 The Asian Games: a full factual text (history from New Delhi 1951, the OCA, host cities, the all-time medal table, and Iraq's record — seven gold medals to 2018 as printed, Ali Adnan Amir, Safaa Rashed, Salwan Jassim). Lesson 8 Two African countries: parallel fact-file texts on Libya and Sudan (area, population, currency, language, geography, products). Round up (p. 85) restates adverbials, passives and present perfect vs past simple. Story time "Together for a better school" (pp. 86–87): Ammar and Fahad rally classmates, families and finally the whole school to clean the school grounds and are made Environment Ambassadors. Project 4 (pp. 88–89): ordering a play script of the Australian folk tale of Rainbow Bird and the fire stick, performance methods (drama, shadow acting, stick puppets, narrate and draw), then performing an own story.

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 90–93).** Lesson 1 Listening: Listening tip 4 (why it is important to listen carefully — information, grammar, and how the speaker feels) with tense-choice and feelings work on three conversations. Lesson 2 Speaking: Speaking tip 5 – Working together, and a question-bank game over the whole book (Graduation party reviews, Travel adventures, The school of the future, A famous career, Two African countries …). Lesson 3 Reading: a mock "Daily Arab News" page (17 August, Issue 547: "POLICE FIND GIRL AFTER THREE HOURS", "SPORTS STAR ARRIVES IN CAIRO") to scan and retell. Lesson 4 Writing: Writing tip 5 – Editing your work, using a correction key (Gr. grammar, P. punctuation, Sp. spelling, wo word order, ww wrong word, ⌒ join, ⋀ word missing) to correct the marked falconry text and re-order its paragraphs.

**Transcripts (pp. 94–110) and Grammar and functions reference (pp. 111–119).** Full transcripts, including the Unit 8 Tariq Rahdi wildlife-photographer talk. The reference gives form/use tables: *There is/are* and *It has/doesn't have*, adjective order (Unit 1); likes/dislikes with gerunds, expressing preferences, making suggestions, present/past question forms (Unit 2); comparatives, superlatives, the second conditional, active and passive forms (Unit 3); past simple and past continuous, future with *will* (Unit 5); *be going to*, *will*, relative pronouns *who/that/which* (Unit 6); adverbs and adverbials, past simple vs present perfect, present and past passive with a verb table (Unit 7). Imprint and photography credits close the book (printed p. 120).

### Grammar spine

Describing places with *There is/are* and *It has/doesn't have* (Unit 1) → adjective order for descriptions (Unit 1) → present continuous vs past continuous for interrupted action (Units 1–2) → compound words (Unit 1) → verbs + gerund for likes/dislikes, and preference/suggestion frames (*I'd rather* + bare infinitive, *How about* + -ing) (Unit 2) → present and past simple question formation in depth (Unit 2) → comparatives, superlatives and *(not) as … as* (Unit 3) → the first conditional for real futures (Unit 3) → **the second conditional for imaginary situations — new in this book** (Unit 3) → *used to* for past-vs-present comparison (Unit 3) → pronouns: subject, object and possessive (Units 3 and 5) → present simple passive with *can* (Unit 3) → past simple vs past continuous as narrative grammar (Unit 5) → future *will* for schedules and certainties, quantifiers *most/least/some/a few* (Unit 5) → *be going to* vs *will* (Unit 6) → defining relative clauses with *who/which/that* for people and things (Unit 6) → the article system *a/an/the/zero* (Unit 6) → adverbials and their word order, Manner–Place–Time (Unit 7) → present perfect vs past simple (Unit 7) → present and past passive across factual texts (Unit 7).

## Grade 9 — English for Iraq, 3rd Intermediate Activity Book (activity book / workbook)

### Identity block

- **Identity verdict vs the record title ("English for Iraq — Activity Book"):** MATCH in substance — the workbook companion to the 3rd Intermediate Student's Book, lesson-for-lesson throughout. **Caveat: this PDF does not contain the book's front matter** (see offset note), so the title-page wording itself could not be inspected in this file; identity rests on the unit/lesson headers, the cross-references to the Student's Book, the shared cast and texts, and the production slug below.
- **Title as printed:** not present in this file (front matter missing). By series pattern it is *English for Iraq — 3rd Intermediate Activity Book*, Republic of Iraq, Ministry of Education; publisher Garnet Publishing Ltd. — the Student's Book of the same set is the 2023 revised edition, and this workbook's embedded production data (below) is consistent with that revision.
- **Authors as printed:** not present in this file. The Student's Book of the set names Terry O'Neill, Peter Snow and Sally Etherton.
- **Edition/year evidence in the file:** the partial text layer carries the production slug "IRAQ_G9_AB_2022_BOOK.indb" with an August 2023 production timestamp — i.e. a book produced in 2022 and printed/revised in the 2023 cycle, matching the Student's Book's "Revised edition © 2023".
- **ISBN as printed:** cannot be verified from this file (no imprint page present).
- **PDF pages:** 124, all read — but they are printed pp. 4–127 of the book. The printed book almost certainly runs to at least p. 128 (the Grade 7 Activity Book ends with a Notes page at p. 128); if the Grade 9 book follows the same pattern, its title/imprint/"This book belongs to" pages (printed pp. 1–3) and any final page are **not in this PDF**.
- **Printed vs PDF offset:** printed page = PDF page + 3 throughout the file (PDF p. 1 = printed p. 4, Unit 1 Lesson 1; PDF p. 124 = printed p. 127, the last Extra activities page).
- **Text-layer character:** partial — a clean digital text layer exists on PDF pp. 1–18 only (Unit 1, printed pp. 4–21); the rest is page images. The whole book was read from 4-up contact sheets, with the Unit 1 text layer as a cross-check.
- **Copy marks:** many pages carry a small third-party header stamp at the top ("Abbas Al-Qurainy / Abbas3rd") — the scan source's mark, not part of the book. Workbook printed in black and white.

### Unit-by-unit summary

The workbook tracks the Student's Book lesson by lesson, adding its own language boxes, extra texts and a large Extra activities bank.

**Unit 1 — Describing things (printed pp. 4–21).** Sentence matching and picture sentences with *There is/isn't/are/aren't*; completing a missing-boy description; a workbook-only text on Istanbul's Cevahir Mall with questions; listening completions and the box "Spoken language" (mother's and children's phrases — *Hurry up, then!*, *I'm just coming*); past continuous completions and a paired diary interview ("What were you doing on Monday at six o'clock?"); a fact-file table for the Panther 3.0D article, word-finding by line number, true/false, and finding adjective–noun pairs with opposites beside two boxes, "Adjectives" (opposites, including *un-* forms) and "quite and really" (quite weakens, really strengthens); building compound words (sweetshop, car park, information desk …); purpose sentences with *so you can/can't*; circling eight adjectives in a "Sofa sale!" advert and matching features to purposes; finding compound words and adjective meanings in the GX43 Smart Watch and LoudHouse speaker adverts; Dania's speaker review (choosing fully/completely, easy/quick, portable/light …); completing Salwa's skateboard email (can, can go, easy to put together, fantastic, help you, saving up, so, well) and writing an own email about a new item; analysing a family description into a Person/Verbs/Adjectives/Nouns table, completing Sally's description, describing Ahmed and planning and writing a description of someone known; email true/false work; the box "Polite language" (softening negatives: *not very good-looking*, *a bit short / not very tall*, *not very good at …*) with rewriting practice; adjective-order multiple choice; smartwatch advert true/false, an advert-elements checklist, and designing an own advert; a Round up (description note tables, opposites, definitions); Story time notes and questions on "Lucky customer 50".

**Unit 2 — Spare time activities (pp. 22–39).** Reema's listening (choosing like/enjoy forms), verb-collocation building (do/go/have/kick/read/score/spend/watch/win + phrases), a like-scale verb table (love, really enjoy, like, don't like, hate) and personal opinion writing; dialogue ordering, completing *to go / go / going* after preference frames, completing the Rasha and Sana conversation, and suggestion role-plays; genre matching and a wordsearch of book-type adjectives, completing a past-tense book review ("The Mountains of the Moon"), and writing about an own favourite book; the Lucy Fisher interview matching, question word-order, and two editing tasks — correcting wrong *information* in one student's write-up (comedy not horror, audition not film test, 200 girls not 300, six months not seven, special teacher) and correcting eight *spelling* mistakes in another (acter, comdy, caracter, where, col, tuk, speshial, hur); note tables for talking about a book or film, same-meaning question matching (What's it called? = What's the title?), and building past simple questions for a conversation about the film *Lost*; matching the STAR! article's sentences to photographs, short answers, completing the article's verbs in the present simple, then retelling the story in the past simple; tables for the graduation party and circus, invitation sentence completion and writing an own invitation email; fact-file definitions and section matching for *The Greatest Showman*, story ordering, and completing a blank fact file for an own favourite film; a Round up (Sara's weekend listening table, collocations, genre definitions); Story time notes on "The champion".

**Unit 3 — Our world (pp. 40–57).** Animal-verb completions; the box "Spelling rule – Final consonants d, g, n, t" (doubling: big → bigger/biggest; final y → i) with comparative sorting; rewriting comparisons three ways (bigger than / not as big as / smaller than); the box "Superlative forms of adjectives – Add est or most" with opinion questions; headline-to-data matching for the growing-world stories, completing the rate/cause table from listening, first conditional multiple choice and sentence finishing, and writing own first conditionals; So/Neither agreement practice (answering speakers, *neither +* phrases, choosing So do I / So am I in a zoo conversation) and personal attitude writing; second conditional work — choosing correct endings for spider scenarios, matching beginnings and endings, completing verb forms, and finishing personal sentences ("If there was a spider on my desk now, I would scream and run home!"); falconry questions; the box "Reading skill – Understanding inferences" applied to the falconry text; identifying they/their/them in an extract and completing the pronoun table (subject/object/possessive adjective), then completing two conversations with pronouns; answering the wildlife questionnaire, matching opinions to reasons, analysing the questionnaire's own structure (title, topic, instructions, questions, situations, answers, points, scores), designing an own questionnaire, and punctuating second conditional questions; the box "The present passive" (be + past participle, when the doer is unknown or unimportant, *by* phrases, *can be found*) with matching, completion, word-order and a six-sentence active-to-passive rewrite about Rawan and a hedgehog; notes and an own Fact File on local life modelled on the marshes text; a Round up of definitions and mixed multiple choice; Story time notes on "How to fill a space".

**Unit 4 — Review (pp. 58–61).** The missing-girl announcement listening (name, age, adjective ticking), the Panther car advertisement listening (price, economy, seats, feature ticking); a workbook-only biography text, "Ibrahim's life story" (a village boy who becomes a zoologist in France), with paragraph-heading ordering and true/false; completing the repeat-request conversations ("What did you say?", "What was that?", "I didn't get that.") — including the "Thirty Days on the Farm / Thirteen Days" misunderstanding; writing an own life story from a staged plan (Introduction; Before you started school; primary; intermediate; leaving). **Extra activities (pp. 62–65)** — a single mixed bank for Units 1–4, exercises 1–10: ten pairs of opposites; question word-order about friends; *to go/go/going* completion; past simple verb forms; completing a story summary ("The Terrible Forest"); pronoun and possessive completion; first conditional multiple choice; sentence rewriting (second conditional stems, comparatives, passives); and a workbook-only true story, "Too few leopards!" — Persian leopards in Iraq down to about 25 in 2022, and brothers Nabaz and Bahaz Faruq Ali photographing them on Bamo mountain — with questions.

**Unit 5 — The media (pp. 66–83).** Sports equipment naming; the football-scores listening table (Brazil–India, Juventus–AC Milan, Manchester United–Arsenal); completing Ali and Fahed's past simple conversation and inventing scores for a role-play; a present→past verb table; Nawal Ramzi true/false, sorting her story into Background events vs Story events, building questions from prompts, and writing a summary from notes about an elephant TV programme; finding what the newspaper numbers refer to, a reporter's notebook for the sports-city article, questions on the Salma/John story, checking the small ads (Cedars, Hassan and Son, Classic Tables), a past simple/continuous text about a French president's visit to Iraq, and choosing do/doing forms in the news stories; matching thirteen newspaper sections to contents; correcting planted spelling and punctuation errors; homophone sentence writing (there/their, new/knew, one/won, wear/where, hear/here); rewriting the "Mona green" tennis article with exactly 4 apostrophes, 2 full stops, 6 capital letters and 1 exclamation mark; correcting ten false statements about Dalia's and Sara's party reviews against the Student's Book, making sentences negative, and a Majid family past-tense text; the bikes question–answer matching, completing a buyer–seller phone call about a video-game player (Basim and Khaled; 25,000 IQD), and role-playing a table-tennis-table sale; timetable matching with *will* start/finish/last sentences and question building; the class survey — tally chart, bar chart and a report using *a few, some, the most/least (popular)*; a Round up (TV conversation table, programme-type definitions, sport collocations); Story time notes on "A powerful lesson for everyone".

**Unit 6 — Jobs and careers (pp. 84–101).** Workplace/job tables from photographs, "someone who …" descriptions for eight jobs (barber, computer programmer, fashion designer, firefighter, mechanic, pharmacist, postal worker, tour guide), verb–noun collocations (cut hair, put out fires, repair cars …) and writing about a wanted job; questions on Layla and Dania, completing Dania's "My plan" with *will* or *is going to*, personal career questions and writing own plans with *going to*; a full-page reply to Mona's blog, stating an own dilemma with two choices, writing an own problem blog post and swapping to write advice; adding relative clauses from a box, true/false on Hassan's entry, choosing *who/which/that* and word-order sentences with relative clauses; article tick/cross and correction, and completing two personal paragraphs (Tikrit/Cambridge interpreter ambitions; a move from Erbil to Mosul) with a/an/the or nothing; directions completion, writing directions on a map (Ridha's house, school, hotel, bookshop, park), following directions to destinations, and writing questions for direction answers; the Ibn al-Haitham vocabulary matching, the box "Paragraphs" (what paragraphs look like; a paragraph is a group of sentences about one topic or idea), predicting biography content, finding linking words and what they refer to, completing text sentences with also/and/but/which/who, and short answers; writing the questions for Bob Pearson's answers, interviewing a real person about their career using the notes chart, and writing the career paragraph; a Round up (articles in a Hayder paragraph, *will/going to*, directions options); Story time notes on "Hard work pays".

**Unit 7 — Other countries (pp. 102–119).** The box "Adjectives from names of countries" (endings -n, -i, -ish, -an, -ian, -ese) with a twelve-country nationality table and sentence completion; *ever/never* questions and answers; completing Saad and Laith's conversation in the present perfect; scanning the customs texts, a transport table, business-hours questions, and writing answers and a paragraph about customs in Iraq using time adverbs; travel-programme true/false and corrections, synonym finding, and a "the?" table for continents, countries, waterways, lakes, deserts, mountains, mountain groups and cities; finding adverbials in the travel texts; the box "Adverbs of manner – Single words and phrases"; completing paragraphs about speaking clearly and politely; answering How? questions with adverbials; heavy word-order practice building Manner–Place–Time sentences; apology-response ticking, completing apology conversations; the box "Past simple and present perfect tenses" (definite past vs indefinite/continuing) with still-in-London judgement questions and tense completion; the box "Present and past passive" with paired present/past passive sentence writing and full-sentence answers on Kia, Indian phones and Iraqi oil; Asian Games answers, true/false against the medal table, and comparison sentences from the 2018 Jakarta–Palembang table (Iraq: 1 gold, 2 silver, 0 bronze, as printed); Libya/Sudan questions and true/false with corrections, then researching and writing a paragraph about another country from a notes frame; a Round up (nationalities, adverb opposites, adjective→adverb); Story time notes on "Together for a better school".

**Unit 8 — Review (pp. 120–123).** Answer frames for the three Unit 8 conversations and note headings for Tariq Rahdi's talk (2 animals, 2 habitats, 2 jobs, 1 place of education, 2 countries, 1 nationality, 2 ways of travelling); completing sentences with suitable adverbials; the recall game (say what you learnt about a word or phrase — Asian Games, Kia Motors, optics, virtual learning …) and writing two questions each on six Student's Book articles; a reading-strategy check (look at the photographs, the headline, the topic sentences) and sorting words under the two Daily Arab News articles, with questions on the Wissam Taha story; an editing task — 17 numbered corrections and a paragraph order for the Student's Book's marked text — a "Remember!" box listing Writing Tips 1–4, and a final free write (a real event, an imaginary story, or a description of a friend). **Extra activities for Units 5–8 close the book at pp. 124–127:** a past-tense TV conversation, the Ali-and-Jassim broken-leg text with pronoun choices, jobs vocabulary (complain, facilities, officer, opportunity, wages …) and writing about a workplace photograph, sorting words under Travel/Geography/Buildings/Agriculture, and a workbook-only life-story text, "Ali's career" — a boy who wants to leave school at 16, discovers veterinary work on a farm holiday, and tops his class — with true/false and Because-answers.

### Grammar spine (as practised and boxed in this workbook)

*There is/are* and describing (Unit 1) → *quite/really* modifiers and adjective order (Unit 1) → present vs past continuous in narrative (Units 1, 5) → compound words and purpose clauses (Unit 1) → polite softening of negatives (Unit 1) → preference frames *to go/go/going* (Unit 2) → present and past simple question formation (Unit 2) → comparatives with the final-consonant doubling rule and superlatives (Unit 3) → first conditional (Unit 3) → So/Neither agreement (Unit 3) → the second conditional (Unit 3) → pronouns and possessive adjectives (Unit 3) → present passive with *can* (Unit 3) → background vs story events (Unit 5) → homophones and newspaper punctuation (Unit 5) → future *will* for timetables and *most/least/some/a few* for survey reporting (Unit 5) → *going to* vs *will* (Unit 6) → relative clauses with *who/which/that* (Unit 6) → articles a/an/the/zero in connected text (Unit 6) → nationality adjectives (Unit 7) → present perfect with *ever/never* and past simple vs present perfect (Unit 7) → adverbials and their word order (Unit 7) → *the* with geographical names (Unit 7) → present and past passive (Unit 7).
